L385: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L386: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L387: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L388: The proposed dosing regimen for naxitamab is 3.0 mg/kg/day on Days 1, 3, and 5 of each 28-day cycle, for a total of 9 mg/kg/cycle, in combination with GM-CSF 250 µg/m2/day on Days -4 to 0 and 500 µg/m2/day on Days 1 to 5 of each 28-day cycle. The primary efficacy endpoint (ORR) of this dosing regimen was evaluated in Study 12-230, Study 201, and Study 2PR01. The safety of this dosing regimen was supported by data from Study 12-230, Study 201, Study 2PR01, Study 11-009, and Study 12-116. Of these studies, 12-230, 11-009 and 12-116 were conducted by Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Centers (MSKCC), while 201 and 2PR01 were conducted by the Applicant.
L389: FDA recommends a dose cap for patients with a body weight (BW) &gt; 50 kg, with a maximum naxitamab dose of 450 mg/cycle. The dose cap is based on the pharmacokinetic characteristics of naxitamab and the relationships between naxitamab exposure and efficacy/safety. Naxitamab exposure is predicted to increase with increasing BW using per kg dosing. The exposure-response relationship for safety suggests higher safety risks (bronchospasm, hypotension, and hypertension) for patients with higher naxitamab exposures, while for efficacy, no significant relationships between naxitamab exposure and ORR/complete response. The exposure-response analysis for efficacy is based on the limited data from the trials and in small number of patients with potential confounding factors (e.g., ADA effect).
L390: The immunogenicity data from studies 12-230, 11-009 and 12-116 conducted by MSKCC are deemed unreliable as the anti-drug-antibody (ADA) assays used in these trials were not validated. Study 201, conducted by the Applicant, using validated ADA assays only tested 23 patients. Therefore, the following postmarket commitment (PMC) will be issued:
L391: *Conduct immunogenicity testing in all patients enrolled in the ongoing Study 201 and any planned clinical trial(s) of naxitamab using the validated assays to adequately characterize the incidences of anti-drug antibody (ADA) and neutralizing antibody against naxitamab. Assess the impact of ADA on the pharmacokinetics (PK), safety and efficacy of naxitamab to inform naxitamab labeling. Refer to the following FDA Guidance for Industry: "Immunogenicity Assessment for Therapeutic Protein Products" available at https://www.fda.gov/downloads/drugs/guidances/ucm338856.pdf.*
L392: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L393: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L394: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L395: ## The Applicant's Position:
L396: The PK data support the use of naxitamab for the treatment of refractory or relapsed high-risk neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow in combination with GM-CSF. The pharmacokinetic evaluations are based on data from subjects treated with naxitamab for high-risk neuroblastoma or other GD2-positive tumors within a clinical trial dose-escalation setting. The pharmacokinetic properties of naxitamab have been characterized by data from single doses and multiple doses across a range of dose regimens (including the proposed dose regimen) as well as from population pharmacokinetic analyses. The analyses included dose-exposure characterization and intrinsic factor evaluation. The proposed dosing regimen was further supported by efficacy and safety evaluations.
L397: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L398: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that the PK data support the use of naxitamab for the treatment of refractory or relapsed high-risk neuroblastoma in bone or bone marrow, in combination with GM-CSF. Of note, only the PK data from Study 201 are deemed reliable because the PK assays used in other studies, including studies 12-230, 11-009, and 12-116, were not fully validated.
L399: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L400: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L401: ## The Applicant's Position:
L402: The proposed dosing regimen was based on a clinical judgement during the dose-escalation part of Trial 12-230 (see Section 8.1.1).
L403: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L404: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position. Trial 12-230 evaluated naxitamab doses of 0.3, 0.6, 0.8, 1.0, 1.2, 1.4, 1.6, 1.8, 2.0, 2.2, 2.4, 2.6, 2.8, 3.0, 3.2 mg/kg QD administered on Day 1, 3, and 5 of each 28-day cycle in the Phase 1 portion of the trial and evaluated naxitamab 3.0 mg/kg QD on Day 1, 3, and 5 of each 28-day cycle in the Phase 2 portion of the trial. Results of this trial suggested that ORR was comparable across the different dose levels in Phase 1 and a higher complete response (CR) rate was observed in the 3 mg/kg group from Phase 2 based on cross-phase comparison. The MTD was not reached during the trial. However, a greater
L405: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L406: percentage of patients in the 2.6 to 3.2 mg/kg dose group reported \ge 1 related Grade 3+ treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAE) (71%, 15/21) and \ge 1 related serious adverse events (SAE) (38%, 8/21) compared to those in patients in the lower dose groups (39%, 14/36 Grade 3+ TEAE; 22%, 8/36 \ge 1 related SAE). The proposed dosing regimen of 3.0 mg/kg (9.0 mg/kg per cycle) is generally acceptable based on benefit-risk evaluation from Trial 12-230, 201, and 2PR01; however, FDA recommends a dose cap for body weight (BW) &gt;50 kg (see Section 6.2.2.2 and Section 6.3.2.2 for detailed information).
L407: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L408: ## The Applicant's Position:
L409: No therapeutic individualization is needed in the proposed indication based on demographic factors and DDI considerations, as further detailed in Sections 6.3.2.3 and 6.3.2.4.
L410: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L411: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that no therapeutic individualization is needed in the proposed indication based on DDI considerations. However, FDA recommends a dose cap for BW &gt;50 kg since naxitamab exposure is predicted to increase with increasing BW using a per kg dosing regimen and the exposure-response relationship for safety suggests higher safety risks (bronchospasm, hypotension, and hypertension) for patients with higher naxitamab exposures.
L412: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L413: ## The Applicant's Position:
L414: There are no outstanding issues.
L415: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L416: FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position of no outstanding clinical pharmacology issues precluding approval of this application. FDA will issue a PMC requesting the Applicant to adequately characterize naxitamab immunogenicity using fully-validated assays in the ongoing Study 201 and any planned clinical trial(s) of naxitamab, and assess the impact of ADA on the PK, safety and efficacy of naxitamab to inform naxitamab labeling.
L417: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L418: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L419: ## The Applicant's Position:
L420: The PK of naxitamab has been evaluated in four clinical trials in patients. MSK evaluated the PK of naxitamab in three trials (Trials 11-009, 12-116, and 12-230) as part of a dose-escalation setting using a partially validated assay developed by MSK. Y-mAbs investigated the PK of naxitamab with the proposed dose regimen in Trial 201 using a fully validated assay. In addition, a population PK analysis of naxitamab was performed using data from these 4 trials to characterize the PK after single and multiple doses in subjects with high-risk neuroblastoma and to explore the impact of subject demographics and intrinsic factors on relevant PK parameters.
L421: Naxitamab is administered by intravenous infusion only. There appeared to be a rapid distribution phase during the first 48 hours after each infusion compared with the observed terminal elimination phase after the third infusion. In the population PK analysis, the terminal half-life was estimated to be 197 hours (8.2 days). Naxitamab exposure is therefore expected to be comparable across cycles with 4 to 8 weeks between cycles. This is also in line with the limited accumulation from one cycle to the next that has been observed in the precycle trough concentration levels in Trial 201.
L422: The dose-escalation trials (Trials 11-009, 12-116, and 12-230) generally showed that naxitamab exposure (C_{max} and AUC) increased approximately linearly with increasing dose within each trial. The population PK analyses confirmed the dose proportionality seen in the individual trial analyses.
L423: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L424: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position on the general pharmacology and pharmacokinetic characteristics of naxitamab. Of note, FDA calculated the terminal half-life of naxitamab based on the PK data from Cycle 1 in Trial 201, as Trial 201 is the only trial that used the fully validated assay in the naxitamab concentration measurement.
L425: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L426: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L427: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L428: ## The Applicant's Position:
L429: The clinical pharmacology program did not comprise specific supportive effectiveness evaluations.
L430: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L431: An exposure-response analysis for efficacy was conducted by the Applicant in response to FDA's information request. FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position that no significant relationships were identified between naxitamab exposure and ORR/CR, as detailed in Section 19.4.2. Of note, the exposure-response analysis examining the relationship between naxitamab exposure and ORR/CR was conducted using limited data from the trials in a small number of patients with potential confounding factors (e.g., ADA effect).
L432: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L433: ## The Applicant's Position:
L434: The proposed dosing regimen has proven effective with manageable side effects in patients with neuroblastoma, as detailed in Sections 8.1 and 8.2. Robustness of the results was further evident from the subgroup analyses, as detailed in Sections 6.3.2.3 and 8.1.8.
L435: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L436: FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's position. No significant relationships between naxitamab exposure and ORR were identified; however, the exposure-response relationship for safety suggests higher safety risks (Grade 2+ bronchospasm, hypotension, and hypertension) for patients with higher naxitamab exposures, as detailed in Sections 19.4.2 and 19.4.3.
L437: The Applicant proposed a naxitamab dosage of 3 mg/kg/day on Days 1, 3, and 5, for a total dose of 9 mg/kg per cycle. Population PK analysis identified the effect of body weight on naxitamab exposure with higher exposure at higher body weights. Naxitamab exposure was simulated with the proposed dosing regimens across the relevant body weight range (10 –
L438: 80 kg) using the final population PK model, as detailed in Section 19.4.1.
L439: **Table 6-1. Summary of simulated naxitamab clearance and AUC0-672h versus bodyweight**

L441: *Source: The Applicant's population PK report (hu3F8-MS-02), Page 59, Table 11.*
L442: As shown in Table 6-1, mean predicted AUC0-672h is nearly doubled in patients with BW 70-80 kg compared to patients with a BW of 20-30 kg. Flat dosing in adults and adolescents with higher BW will minimize PK variability. There is limited clinical safety experience in patients with BW &gt; 50 kg at naxitamab doses &gt; 450 mg per cycle (i.e., 3 patients with BW &gt;50 kg received a dose &gt;450 mg/cycle, with 2 patients receiving 3 infusions and 1 patient receiving 5 infusions). Patients with BW &gt;50 kg receiving 3 mg/kg dose are expected to have higher exposures than that observed in the clinical trials. Therefore, based on exposure-matching, the recommended dosage of naxitamab is 3 mg/kg/day up to 150 mg per day on Days 1, 3, and 5, for a total dose of 9 mg/kg up to 450 mg per cycle.
L443: ## 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L444: ## The Applicant's Position:
L445: On the basis of the assessment of a potential impact of intrinsic factors on the PK of naxitamab, no dose adjustment or change in dosing regimen is required to accommodate demographic characteristics or special populations.
L446: The effect of intrinsic factors such as body weight, body mass index, body surface area, lean body mass, race, gender, age, and renal or hepatic function was evaluated in the population PK analysis (Study hu3F8-MS-02). Only body weight was retained as a significant covariate, and exposure was predicted to increase with increasing body weight. However, an assessment of
L447: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L448: TEAEs, infusion-related TEAEs, and SAEs by body weight (&lt;20 kg, 20 to &lt;50 kg, and ≥50 kg) did not indicate increasing toxicities with increasing body weight. In addition, no safety concerns emerged from analyses of TEAEs across gender, race, prior anti-GD-2 therapy, or ADA status (Section 8.2.7).
L449: Finally, efficacy analyses across subgroups by baseline disease status (primary refractory disease vs. incomplete response to salvage treatment), disease compartment (bone vs. bone marrow), age, body weight, prior GD2 therapy, and ADA status have shown consistent efficacy of naxitamab, which supports the premise that no dose adjustments are required based on demographics or for special populations (Section 8.1.8).
L450: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L451: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position that no dose adjustments are required based on demographics or for special populations. FDA recommends a dosing cap (150 mg per day and 450 mg per cycle) for patients with BW &gt;50 kg as described above in Section 6.3.2.2.
L452: In addition, the FDA disagrees with the Applicant's position regarding ADA status since immunogenicity data from studies 12-230, 11-009 and 12-116 conducted by MSKCC are deemed unreliable as the anti-drug-antibody (ADA) assays used in these trials were not validated. Study 201, conducted by the Applicant using validated ADA assays, only evaluated ADA in 23 patients. As such, the FDA will issue a PMC to gather additional ADA and PK data to adequately assess the incidence of ADA and the impact of ADA status on the PK, safety and efficacy of naxitamab.
L453: 6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?
L454: ## The Applicant's Position:
L455: No formal drug-drug interaction studies have been performed. This is considered acceptable because it is unlikely that naxitamab will interact with concomitant drugs in a way that will affect the systemic exposure of either naxitamab or concomitant drugs for the following reasons:
L456: - While nonprotein drugs are metabolized via the cytochrome P-450 system and/or renal or biliary excretion, an antibody like naxitamab, being a protein, is eliminated via different mechanisms: cellular uptake followed by proteolytic degradation and via
L457: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L458: interaction between antibody and its target.
L459: - Metabolic competition between antibodies and other proteins have not been described. Furthermore, interaction at the binding target (GD2) between nonprotein drugs or other protein drugs and naxitamab is unlikely.
L460: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L461: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.

Given several limitations of the other trials, including use of a different dose or regimen of naxitamab, use in patients with different tumor types, or in the case of Study 2PR01, the retrospective nature of the data, information derived from these trials were supportive only.

L480: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L481: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L482: ## The Applicant's Position:
L483: The efficacy evaluation for the BLA is based on an interim analysis of all efficacy data from the pivotal Trial 12-230 at the data cutoff date. Trial 201 and the retrospective Study 2PR01 provide supporting efficacy data.
L484: - Subjects could enroll to Trial 12-230 more than once and could have different disease profiles at different enrollments. Therefore, they are counted by enrollment for the efficacy analysis, and the term “enrollments” is used in place of “subjects.”
- Trial 201 provides interim data for enrollments with at least 1 follow-up for assessment of efficacy endpoints. Subjects could only enroll once in this trial.
- The retrospective Study 2PR01 provides medical record data from patients treated with naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF under compassionate use in Spain.
L485: ## 8.1.1. Trial 12-230
L486: ## The Applicant's Description:
L487: ## Trial Design
L488: ## Basic Trial Design
L489: The pivotal Trial 12-230 was an open-label, single-center trial including subjects with either primary refractory disease or incomplete response to salvage treatment. The trial also included subjects with other disease status at baseline, but they were not included in the efficacy analysis.
L490: Trial 12-230 had two phases. Phase 1 was a single-center, single-arm, open-label, 3 + 3 dose-escalation design. Phase 2 was a single-center, single-arm, nonrandomized, open-label trial.
L491: Phase 1 assessed escalating doses of intravenous (i.v.) naxitamab infusions (Days 1, 3, 5) in combination with subcutaneous (s.c.) GM-CSF (Day -4 through 5) during a 10-day cycle repeated every 2 to 4 weeks. Subjects with no dose-limiting toxicity (as defined in protocol) and no progression of disease during the first cycle had the option of receiving an additional 3 cycles if antidrug antibody (ADA) titer was negative. Subjects who completed 4 cycles of treatment
L492: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L493: without complications or disease progression had the option of continuing treatment for up to 24 months from their first dose of naxitamab.
L494: In Phase 2 all subjects receiving naxitamab at 9.0 mg/kg per cycle. Dosing with naxitamab occurred on Days 1, 3, and 5 of each cycle in the presence of s.c. GM-CSF (Days -4 through 5). Cycles were repeated monthly through 5 cycles, then every 1 to 2 months for up to 24 months from trial enrollment or until a subject had received five cycles after a CR or PR was achieved, whichever came first. The basic Phase 2 dosing design is shown in Figure 8-1. The timing of naxitamab and GM-CSF administration was the same in Phase 1.
L495: **Figure 8-1 Basic Trial Design**
L496: ## Trial Location
L497: The trial is a single-center trial conducted in New York City, New York, United States.
L498: ## Choice of Control Group
L499: Since high-risk neuroblastoma is a rare disease, the trial is single-arm with no comparator.
L500: ## Diagnostic Criteria
L501: The trial enrolled subjects with neuroblastoma diagnosed by 1) histopathology or 2) bone marrow metastases or MIBG-avid lesion(s) plus high urine catecholamine levels.
L502: ## Key Inclusion/Exclusion Criteria
L503: Enrollment was open to subjects older than 1 year of age with a diagnosis of neuroblastoma, as defined in the section on diagnostic criteria above. Subjects must have had high-risk neuroblastoma (including MYCN-amplified stage 2/3/4/4s of any age and MYCN-nonamplified Stage 4 in subjects greater than 18 months or age). Phase 1 subjects must have had refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma that was resistant to standard therapy (standard therapy included
L504: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L505: intensive induction chemotherapy, followed by a variety of consolidation or salvage therapies, depending on response). Phase 2 subjects must have had primary or secondary refractory disease in bone or bone marrow, defined as morphologic evidence of neuroblastoma in bone marrow and/or abnormal ^{123}\text{I}-MIBG uptake in osteomedullary sites, or had to be in second or greater complete response as defined by INRC with ^{123}\text{I}-MIBG findings quantitated by Curie scores. Prior treatment with other anti-GD2 antibody therapies, including murine 3F8, naxitamab, ch14.18, and hu14.18 was allowed. No chemotherapy or immunotherapy for a minimum of 3 weeks prior to start of naxitamab was allowed. Moreover, white blood cell (WBC) count greater than or equal to 1000/\mu\text{L}, absolute neutrophil count greater than or equal to 500/\mu\text{L}, absolute lymphocyte count greater than or equal to 500/\mu\text{L}, and platelet count greater than or equal to 25,000/\mu\text{L} were required.
L506: Subjects were excluded for existing major organ dysfunction greater than CTCAE Grade 2, with the exception of hearing loss and hematologic toxicity (defined as suppression of all subtypes of WBCs, red blood cells, and platelets); active life-threatening infection; history of allergy to mouse proteins; anti-human anti-GD2 antibody (ADA) titer greater than 1,300 ELISA U/mL, and history of allergy to GM-CSF.
L507: ## Dose Selection
L508: Phase 1 assessed escalating doses of intravenous naxitamab infusions (Days 1, 3, 5) in combination with s.c. GM-CSF (Day -4 through 5). Dose levels were 0.3 to 3.2 mg/kg/d (0.9 to 9.6 mg/kg per cycle). Subjects came off trial if they had a dose-limiting toxicity, as defined in the protocol, during the first cycle.
L509: Although a maximum tolerated dose was not attained in Phase 1, 9.0 mg/kg per cycle was selected for further development and implemented in Phase 2 of the trial: Intravenous naxitamab infusions (Days 1, 3, 5) of 3.0 mg/kg per infusion in combination with s.c. GM-CSF (Day -4 through 5). The choice of 3.0 mg/kg per infusion (9.0 mg/kg per cycle) as the optimal dose for the Phase 2 part and for further clinical development of naxitamab was a clinical judgement based on the evaluation of the clinical pharmacokinetics, efficacy, safety, and tolerability at that time (i.e., including the investigator's clinical judgement of each subject case based on individual subject details).
L510: ## Trial Treatments, Treatment Assignment, and Blinding
L511: Trial 12-230 was an open-label, single-arm trial.
L512: Each subject received naxitamab with GM-CSF over a 10-day period in a cycle. A cycle started with 5 days of GM-CSF administered s.c. at 250 \mu\text{g}/\text{m}^2/\text{d} before the start of naxitamab infusions
L513: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L514: (Day –4 to Day 0) (Figure 8-1). On Days 1 through 5, GM-CSF was administered at a dose of 500 \mu\text{g}/\text{m}^2/\text{d}.
L515: On Days 1, 3, and 5, naxitamab was infused i.v. over approximately 30 minutes.
L516: In Phase 1, cycles were repeated at approximately 2- to 4-week intervals through Cycle 4, and subjects who completed 4 cycles of treatment without complication or disease progression had the option of continuing treatment for up to 24 months from their first dose of naxitamab. According to protocol, Phase 1 dose escalation ranged from 0.9 mg/kg per cycle to 10.8 mg/kg per cycle; however, dose escalation stopped at 9.6 mg/kg per cycle.
L517: In Phase 2, cycles were repeated monthly though 5 cycles, and Group 1 and Group 3 subjects had the option of continuing treatment every 1 to 2 months for up to 24 months from trial enrollment or until they had received 5 cycles after a major response (CR or PR) was achieved, whichever came first. In Phase 2, the selected total dose per cycle was 9.0 mg/kg (3.0 mg/kg per infusion). See Section 6.2.2.1 for details.
L518: ## Dose Modification, Dose Discontinuation
L519: For adverse events (AEs) suspected to be related to naxitamab, management guidance and dose modifications were provided in the protocol. In the dose-escalation phase, Phase 1, subjects came off trial if they had a dose-limiting toxicity during the first cycle. In Phase 2, subjects were discontinued from naxitamab if their ADA titers exceeded a cutoff limit (&gt;1300 U/mL), but could resume treatment if their ADA titer dropped below the cutoff limit. Investigators could discontinue trial drug for AEs associated with trial drug, including unacceptable toxicity or exacerbation of underlying disease.
L520: ## Administrative Structure
L521: Trial oversight was managed as follows:
L522: - Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center (MSK) served as the sponsor of the trial and was responsible for clinical trial management and data monitoring.
- Y-mAbs contracted clinical research associates who were independent of the trial to conduct on-site and remote data monitoring for the data used in the BLA.
- Y-mAbs commissioned the development of an electronic data capture system, and data entry support, data management oversight, and statistical analyses were performed by a contract research organization (CRO).
L523: All laboratory assessments, including PK and ADA assessments, were conducted by MSK laboratories.
L524: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L525: In addition, independent efficacy assessments were contracted by Y-mAbs: blinded, external, independent reviews of medical images and of pathology slides were conducted by board-certified radiologists and board-certified pathologists, respectively.
L526: ## Procedures and schedule
L527: Bilateral bone marrow aspirates and biopsies and imaging (CT, MRI, MIBG scan, and/or PET) for response evaluation were performed before treatment, during treatment before Cycle 3, and after Cycle 5 (Phase 1), and approximately every 3 months during Phase 2 and during follow-up. Complete history, physical exam, blood pressure, hematology, biochemistry, liver and kidney laboratory tests, urinalysis (Phase 1 only), pregnancy test, echocardiography (Phase 1 only), and electrocardiogram (Phase 1 only) were conducted prior to treatment and where relevant during treatment and follow-up. Blood for ADA assessment was drawn before each treatment cycle. Adverse events were recorded continuously and up to 30 days after the last treatment or until the subject began a new treatment, whichever was earlier.
L528: ## Trial Endpoints
L529: The BLA is submitted for Accelerated Approval based on the surrogate endpoint of overall response rate (ORR), defined as the sum of partial (PR) and complete responses (CR), supported by duration of response (DoR), which is defined as the interval between first response and progressive disease. ORR is recognized as an acceptable endpoint supporting accelerated approval for single-arm trials, as discussed and agreed with the FDA during a pre-IND meeting.
L530: The response to therapy for neuroblastoma was assessed by the International Neuroblastoma Response Criteria (INRC), according to the revised INRC updated in 2017.[15]
L531: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L532: The statistical analyses for the interim analysis were defined in the protocol. A standalone statistical analysis plan (SAP) was made. There is only one version of the SAP. The SAP was signed after the statistical analyses of efficacy had started. The principal features in the SAP followed the ones in the protocol. The SAP deviated from the protocol in the following key features:
L533: - Additional data (e.g., demographics, baseline characteristics, safety, efficacy subgroups) and corresponding analyses, to provide and facilitate an overall interpretation of the results, were specified in the SAP.
- The per protocol set analyses were not included in the SAP.
L534: The analyses performed followed the SAP.
L535: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L536: ## Efficacy Analysis
L537: In Phase 2, subjects were enrolled into one of three subject groups described as follows:
L538: - Group 1: Subjects with primary refractory disease (no prior relapse, but incomplete response to treatment) in bone and/or bone marrow, as documented by histology and or ^{123}\text{I}-MIBG scan.
- Group 2: Subjects in second CR or very good partial response and risk for another relapse. The VGPR criterion was removed from Group 2 enrollment criteria with Amendment 20.
- Group 3: Subjects with secondary refractory disease (prior relapse and incomplete response to retrieval therapy) in bone and/or bone marrow as documented by histology and/or ^{123}\text{I}-MIBG. (Note: hereafter this will be referred to as incomplete response to salvage treatment.)
L539: Subjects from Group 2 had no tumor at baseline, therefore, this subject population is not part of the efficacy population but only contributes to the safety population.
L540: The two efficacy analysis groups are the FAS (full analysis set) and PAS (primary analysis set). Both groups enrolled subjects with baseline disease defined in Groups 1 and 3 (primary refractory disease and incomplete response to salvage therapy, had evaluable disease at baseline, and began at least one infusion of naxitamab). The PAS was restricted to enrollments treated with greater than or equal to 3.0 mg/kg per infusion (cycle total of greater than or equal to 9.0 mg/kg), completing at least one cycle of naxitamab, irrespective of trial phase. The SAF (safety analysis set) was for the safety analysis and included all subjects that began an infusion of naxitamab.
L541: Subgroup analyses based on relevant demographic and prognostic factors for efficacy evaluation were prespecified in the SAP.
L542: ## Primary efficacy endpoint
L543: The primary efficacy endpoint for the interim analysis of the pivotal Trial 12-230 was the ORR calculated along with a 95% confidence interval using the Clopper-Pearson exact methodology. As agreed at the pre-IND meeting with FDA, effectiveness was concluded if the lower limit of the exact 95% confidence interval was above 20%.
L544: ## Secondary efficacy endpoint
L545: The duration of response (DoR) was estimated using Kaplan-Meier methods and presented in a Kaplan-Meier plot. DoR was censored at the date of last disease assessment in subjects who did
L546: not experience PD. As a conservative sensitivity analysis, DoR was also analyzed with descriptive summary statistics in which the subjects were considered to have progressed at last disease assessment.
L547: ## *Lack of evaluable disease at baseline*
L548: In line with International Council for Harmonisation E9 guidance (Section 5.2.1), subjects who lacked evaluable disease at baseline, as determined by the independent reviewers, were excluded from the efficacy analysis sets. All subjects in the Trial 12-230 Primary Analysis Set [PAS] had a completed baseline response assessment.
L549: ## Safety Analysis
L550: Safety analyses are defined and presented in Section 8.2.
L551: ## PK Analysis
L552: PK analysis is defined and presented in Section 6.2.1.
L553: ## Protocol Amendments
L554: The trial protocol was amended 22 times during the trial. None of the implemented changes impacted the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the results. Key features of key amendments are summarized in the table below.
L555: Amendment Number	Effective Date	Change

A break between cycles after Cycle 4 of up to approximately 2 months was allowed.

5	21 Oct 2013	Introduced that subjects can continue up to 24 months on naxitamab.

Subjects with recurrent disease that can be completely eradicated without systemic therapy can stay in the trial.

After Cycle 2, only Grade 2 unexpected related AEs will be recorded.

L558: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L559: FDA generally agrees with the description of the trial provided by the Applicant with the exception of the definition of the efficacy population. FDA modified the proposed efficacy population to exclude additional patients based on the dose of naxitamab received, presence of progressive disease at baseline, radiation exposure to target lesions, lack of evaluable disease on central review, and timing of baseline disease assessment relative to completion of prior systemic therapy. Also, although the trial permitted patients to enroll multiple times in different cohorts, FDA considered only the first enrollment by each patient. The efficacy population as determined by FDA consisted of 38 unique patients, compared to 43 unique patients and 45 total enrollments in the Applicant's analysis.
L560: FDA deemed it appropriate to revise the Applicant's definition of the efficacy population to include patients with baseline evaluable disease who initiated at least one infusion of naxitamab (as opposed to having completed at least one cycle) and received greater than or equal to 3.0 mg/kg per infusion. Two patients in the FDA efficacy analysis set received 3.2 mg/kg per infusion. For patients who enrolled in Study 12-230 more than once and received naxitamab dosages within this range during each enrollment, data
L561: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L562: from the first enrollment was used rather than including response data from multiple enrollments for a given patient.
L563: FDA notes that patients with progressive disease at the time of enrollment in the study (patients [REDACTED](b) (6) and [REDACTED](b) (6)) were excluded from the Applicant's efficacy population, consistent with the eligibility criteria for Trial 201 as described in Section 8.1.3. Upon further review of the patients included in the efficacy population, FDA noted that several patients had received radiation to target lesions, and in some cases to the only sites of evaluable disease. Patients enrolled in Trial 12-230 were permitted to receive radiation as medically necessary as determined by the investigator and these patients had no disease evaluable for response determination; therefore FDA excluded these patients from the efficacy population to permit a more accurate assessment of the efficacy of naxitamab. This resulted in an efficacy population of 38 unique patients, compared to 43 unique patients and 45 enrollments in the Applicant's analysis.

L591: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L592: ## The Applicant's Position:
L593: Most protocol deviations were minor and concerned missed assessments, assessments out of window, and issues that were included in later protocol amendments (e.g., naxitamab dosing past that allowed by the protocol at the time, providing informed consent by phone for potential subjects with travel issues).
L594: Nineteen subjects had at least one protocol deviation that was considered major. The type of major deviations fell into three types: informed consent, safety, and trial medication administration. Events reported as major protocol deviations included the following:
L595: - Delays in reporting serious adverse events (SAEs) to the IRB (6 subjects)
- Blood draws for pretreatment assessment before signing consent (1 subject)
- Signature page of the consent having the incorrect amendment number (3 subjects)
- Trial medication administration issues that included the following:Period between GM-CSF administration and start of naxitamab infusion (4 subjects)
- Changes in GM-CSF administration days (2 subjects)
- Missed or incorrect dose of GM-CSF (3 subjects)
- Discontinuation of GM-CSF (2 subjects)
- Change in dosing date for naxitamab (1 subject)
- Multiple interruptions to naxitamab administration since the eCRF could only capture one (1 subject)
L596: Four enrollments having major protocol deviations were part of the PAS group. No subjects were excluded from the PAS group due to major protocol deviations.
L597: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L598: FDA agrees with the description of protocol violations/deviations in 12-230 based on the information provided. The protocol violations/deviations described above are not expected to have a significant impact **on the validity of the safety or efficacy analyses for Trial 12-30.**

L603: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L604: FDA requested a listing of the timing of receipt of all prior therapies and the specific therapies received in order to understand the definition of the efficacy population, which excluded patients with progressive disease. The patients with relapsed disease in the efficacy population in Trial 12-230 (n=21) had received a median of 2 lines of systemic therapy (range 1 to 4). The response to the most recent prior therapy was not uniformly reported.
L605: FDA also asked for a comparison of the eligibility criteria for Trial 201, which was more specific, to the efficacy population in Trial 12-230. Of the patients included in FDA's efficacy analysis for Study 12-230, the following patient would not meet eligibility criteria for Study 201:
L606: - o Patient (b) (6): Patient would likely violate eligibility criterion requiring initiation of GM-CSF within 2 months of diagnosis of relapse, though the exact date of relapse was not recorded.
L607: The remaining patients included in the efficacy population for Trial 12-230 meet the eligibility criteria for Trial 201.
L608: FDA requested additional, detailed information regarding prior therapy and the timing of receipt of such therapy received by patients included in the efficacy population in order to better characterize the study population and any potential impact on the efficacy evaluation. The Applicant provided a detailed listing of therapies received, the response to such therapies as available, and the timing with respect to enrollment on Trial 12-230. L625: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L626: ## The Applicant's Position:
L627: ## Treatment Compliance
L628: All investigational products were prepared and administered in-clinic. Naxitamab compliance was calculated as the difference between the calculated planned dose (last recorded predose body weight x dose level in mg/kg) and the actual dose recorded in the subject CRFs. Calculated compliance for the first cycle averaged 100.1% (\pm 2.92 with a median of 99.9%). Over 25% of the infusions were within \pm 1\% of the calculated dose, with 80.8% of the infusions being within \pm 5\% of the calculated dose and 96.0% of the infusion within \pm 10\%. Individual compliance calculations ranged from 124.7% to 25.8%.
L629: ## Concomitant Medications
L630: Concomitant systemic therapy for neuroblastoma was not allowed during the trial.
L631: However, subjects who developed an ADA titer greater than or equal to 1300 U/mL and therefore, could not have timely treatments with naxitamab and GM-CSF were eligible to receive low-dose maintenance regimens such as irinotecan alone, temozolomide alone, irinotecan-temozolomide, or cyclophosphamide-topotecan. They could also be treated with anti-ADA agents such as rituximab and cyclophosphamide. Naxitamab and GM-CSF treatments could be resumed when the ADA titer fell below 1300 U/mL.
L632: Pretreatment was allowed before naxitamab infusions with analgesics for pain (e.g., hydromorphone) and antihistamines (e.g., diphenhydramine or hydroxyzine).
L633: No major protocol deviations due to the use of prohibited concomitant medications were reported.
L634: ## Rescue medication
L635: Surgical and radiotherapy was allowed for subjects whose neuroblastoma progressed. It was at the discretion of the investigator to decide if naxitamab treatment was to be continued.
L636: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L637: Additional information was requested regarding the use of chemotherapy for patients
L638: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L639: who developed an ADA titer greater than or equal to 1300 U/mL on treatment. Six of 43 patients in the efficacy population had a positive ADA test on treatment and one received systemic therapy on study. This patient received rituximab (with celecoxib premedication) and cyclophosphamide and did not demonstrate an objective response. Therefore, the reviewer is not concerned that receipt of chemotherapy administered to patients who had a positive ADA contributed to any objective responses observed in this trial based on the information provided.
L640: FDA notes that radiation was permitted in Trial 12-230 as clinically indicated after cycle 1. Some patients received radiation to sites of disease prior to the onset of an objective response; patients included in the efficacy analysis who received radiation are listed in Table 8-7 below. Based on receipt of radiation to some target lesions, the best overall response for some patients were reassessed as a PR compared to the prior assessment of CR. Areas of overlap between sites of target lesions and sites of radiation therapy are highlighted in yellow below. Radiation was administered soon after treatment was initiated in all cases. As summarized in the table, based on the information available, this reviewer considers that the observed objective responses cannot be attributed to administration of radiation in these patients; however, administration of radiation may have prevented progression in the irradiated lesions, which would have resulted in an overall response by INRC of disease progression. Concomitant administration of radiation must be considered when interpreting the efficacy results of the trial.

L653: ## The Applicant's Position:
L654: ## Overall Response Rate (ORR)
L655: FDA deleted this section based on revision of the efficacy population after discussion with the Applicant.

L661: ## **Review Issue: Assessment of Overall Response per INRC**
L662: Individual components of the responses used assess the response to naxitamab using the revised INRC (measurement of soft tissue lesions, bone marrow evaluations, and MIBG/FDG evaluations) were independently reviewed and the charters for the independent review of the components of response was included in the BLA; however, the overall assessment of response based on the INRC was made by the Applicant based on the independently reviewed components. During the review, FDA requested detailed listings including detailed information about the inclusion of soft tissue lesions in the overall assessment of response and evaluated the overall response determination per INRC made by the Applicant.
L663: *Reviewer assessment: The reviewer considered that the overall response per INRC as determined by the Applicant was acceptable in the context of this trial.*
L664: ## **Review Issue: Assessment of Soft Tissue Lesions in Overall Response Assessment**
L665: The Applicant provided an overview of response assessment in soft tissue lesions. The Applicant stated that exclusion of patients with soft tissue disease at baseline was at the investigator's discretion and that patients with soft tissue disease expected to be scar tissue were permitted to enroll. Baseline soft tissue disease was followed by CT/MRI and evaluated independently, but the external radiology review was not asked to assess whether disease represented active tumor,
L666: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L667: and MIBG/PET results were not considered in the assessment of soft tissue disease based on the independent review charter.
L668: *Reviewer note: The revised INRC assessment of soft tissue disease incorporates use of MIBG/FDG for assessment of response and defining target lesions.*
L669: Local radiotherapy for soft tissue disease was permitted during the trial. Although the appearance of new soft tissue lesions, or progression in existing soft tissue disease, were incorporated in the overall assessment per INRC, responses in baseline soft tissue lesions were not incorporated into the overall response assessment by INRC because they were not considered active disease and therefore were not considered 'target lesions.' Administration of local radiation to sites of soft tissue disease is not expected to contribute to the responses observed given that these sites were not considered target lesions and only progression (and not responses) in soft tissue would result in a change in overall assessment of response.
L670: Below is a summary of each patient with soft tissue disease at baseline or during the trial, and an assessment of how this impacted FDA's assessment of overall response.
L671: - (b) (6): there was a soft tissue lesion (STL) considered not evaluable at baseline, and a new STL was detected on (b) (6) and based on independent imaging and pathology review PD in bone or bone marrow on the same date. No consequences for overall best response.
- (b) (6): STL at baseline; 19.1 mm shrinking to 12.4 mm. Based on independent imaging and pathology review the patient had CR in bone and bone marrow. No consequences for overall best response.
L672: *Reviewer note: The patient is noted to have a CR according to independent response listings. The sponsor provided information to support their assertion that the soft tissue lesion was not considered active tumor, namely that the area was not MIBG-avid at baseline and was interpreted by independent imaging as "liver lesion probably post treatment."*

The sponsor states that the soft tissue disease was assessed as scar tissue and response in the soft tissue disease was not included in the assessment of response.*

- (b) (6): This patient received radiation to the abdomen (soft tissue) and femur shortly after initiation of treatment with naxitamab. Reported response is CR and FDA agrees, considering only bone marrow as the target lesion.
L676: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L677: - (b) (6): This patient received radiation to the abdomen (soft tissue) shortly after initiation of treatment with naxitamab. Reported response is PR and FDA agrees, considering bone and bone marrow as target lesions.

Reviewer note: *The patient has a reported PR. At 6 months post onset of response, the patient demonstrated a CR in bone, but there was no bone marrow assessment (overall response: NE). Given the incomplete assessment at 6 months, the patient's proven duration of response was conservatively considered to be 3 months by FDA, not 6 months as reported by the Applicant.*

- (b) (6): This patient received radiation to the pancreatic bed and nodes (soft tissue) shortly after initiation of treatment with naxitamab. Reported response is CR and FDA agrees, considering bone and bone marrow as target lesions.

- (b) (6): This patient received radiation to the abdomen (soft tissue) shortly after initiation of treatment with naxitamab. Reported response is PR, and FDA agrees considering bone marrow only.
L678: *The reviewer considers that radiation is not expected to contribute to the overall responses observed. Ultimately, based on the information provided, it appears the approach of not including soft tissue responses in the INRC assessment, considering that these lesions are not assessed as active tumor (i.e., were not considered target lesions), is acceptable.*
L679: ## Review Issue: Duration between Prior Therapy and Baseline Disease Assessment
L680: FDA considered whether the short time from completion of systemic therapy until the baseline disease assessment could confound the assessment of response to naxitamab. In practice, the urgency to enroll patients, particularly with primary refractory disease who may have demonstrated stable disease, minor response or partial response to prior intensive chemotherapy, onto a clinical trial may render a standard washout period from prior therapy (i.e., at least 4 weeks prior to baseline disease assessment) challenging. Since patients with progressive disease were excluded from the trial, patients with relapsed disease were treated first with other therapy (typically chemotherapy or chemotherapy and immunotherapy) prior to enrollment, thus the concern that the effect of prior therapy is not reflected in the baseline disease assessment (and thus that responses observed in the trial cannot wholly be attributed to naxitamab) is greater in this population.
L681: FDA performed a sensitivity analysis excluding patients (non-responders and responders) from the efficacy population who had a baseline scan &lt; 2.5 weeks from the time of completion of systemic therapy. In Trial 12-230, this criterion applied to 5 responders and 4 non-responders,
L682: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L683: resulting in 9 responders among 30 patients (ORR 30%), slightly lower than the ORR without the sensitivity analysis.
L684: *Because this sensitivity analysis excluded responding patients with durable complete responses which the review team did not consider reasonably attributable to prior therapy, FDA decided to only exclude those patients who received systemic therapy up until the time of the baseline scan (i.e., those patients with a time from systemic therapy to baseline scan of zero weeks) from the efficacy population; this applied to one patient in the efficacy population, patient [REDACTED] (b) (6).*

L714: Interpretation of duration of response is limited by the lack of systematic follow-up of responders after discontinuation of treatment with naxitamab. The protocol did not require continued assessments of response after end of treatment, patients may have been followed at another institution, and some received subsequent anti-cancer therapy prior to demonstration of progression. FDA inquired if a later data cut-off for evaluation of duration of response was available for a more mature characterization of DOR, but given the limitations in study assessments and data collection, additional data was not available. It is therefore uncertain whether these limitations have resulted in an underestimation of duration of response.
L715: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L716: ## Dose/Dose Response
L717: ## The Applicant's Position:
L718: In an analysis of Trial 12-230 by dose level (\ge 3.0 mg/kg/infusion (45 enrollments (PAS)) vs &lt; 3.0 mg/kg/infusion (15 enrollments)) the ORRs appeared similar between the two groups. However, importantly the CR rate was higher in the \ge 3.0 mg/kg dose group compared to the &lt; 3.0 mg dose group (31.1% vs 20.0%). Achieving a CR is of major clinical significance. Based on the information above, the dosing regimen of 9 mg/kg per cycle was determined to present the best balance between a manageable safety profile while ensuring effect of naxitamab treatment.
L719: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L720: FDA notes that the data presented regarding the efficacy of doses &lt; 3.0 mg/kg/dose is limited and should be interpreted with caution.

L742: ## 8.1.3. Trial 201
L743: ## The Applicant's Description:
L744: ## Trial Design
L745: Trial 201 is an ongoing, multicenter, international, single-arm, nonrandomized, open-label, Phase 2 trial to evaluate the safety and efficacy of naxitamab administered with GM-CSF in high-risk neuroblastoma subjects with primary refractory disease or incomplete response to salvage treatment in bone and/or bone marrow. In contrast to Trial 12-230, subjects could only be enrolled once in Trial 201.
L746: In Trial 201, subjects underwent the same naxitamab treatment cycles as described for Trial 12-230. Treatment cycles were repeated every 4 weeks (\pm 1 week) until CR or PR followed by five additional cycles every 4 weeks (\pm 1 week). Subsequent cycles could be repeated every
L747: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L748: 8 weeks (\pm2 weeks) through 101 weeks from first infusion at the discretion of the investigator. After end of treatment (EOT), each subject will enter a long-term follow-up for 3 years in which they will be monitored at the referral site.
L749: In agreement with the FDA, Trial 201 data were based on an interim analysis and provide support that the efficacy results obtained in Trial 12-230 are reproducible. However, the interim analysis was not statistically powered to demonstrate efficacy.
L750: ## Trial Location
L751: The trial is being carried out at three sites: Two sites in the United States (9 subjects) and one site in Spain, Europe (16 subjects).
L752: ## Choice of Control Group
L753: Since high-risk neuroblastoma is a rare disease, the trial is single-arm with no comparator.
L754: ## Diagnostic Criteria
L755: The trial enrolled subjects with neuroblastoma diagnosed by 1) histopathology of tumor biopsy or 2) bone marrow aspirate or biopsy indicative of neuroblastoma by histology plus high urine catecholamine levels or MYCN amplification or MIBG-avid lesion(s).
L756: ## Key Inclusion/Exclusion Criteria
L757: Enrollment was open to subjects older than 1 year of age with a diagnosis of neuroblastoma, as defined in the section on diagnostic criteria above. Subjects should have high-risk neuroblastoma with either primary refractory disease or incomplete response to salvage treatment, evaluable in bone and/or bone marrow; if disease was only present in bone, the subject should have evaluable disease outside the radiation areas for being eligible.
L758: The subject should have acceptable hematological status and acceptable liver and kidney function as defined in the protocol.
L759: Subjects were excluded for any systemic anticancer therapy, including chemotherapy or immunotherapy within 3 weeks before the first dose of GM-CSF. Subjects were also excluded for evaluable neuroblastoma outside bone and bone marrow sites.
L760: High-risk neuroblastoma (NB) was defined as INRG [15, 16] MYCN-amplified any INRG stage of any age, or MYCN-nonamplified with INRG Stage M subjects, diagnosed at 18 months of age or older.
L761: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L762: Primary refractory disease was defined as no relapse or PD, but incomplete metastatic response (SD, MR, PR defined by INRC) to treatment in bone and/or BM as documented either by histologic evidence of NB in bone or BM and/or abnormal ^{123}\text{I}-MIBG uptake in skeletal sites. In addition, at trial enrollment, subjects could not be more than 18 months from initiation of chemotherapy and must have received at least 4 cycles of standard induction chemotherapy for high-risk NB.
L763: Incomplete response to salvage treatment was defined as relapse or PD, and incomplete response (SD, MR, PR, but not PD, defined by INRC) to salvage chemotherapy or MIBG therapy immediately before enrollment. Furthermore, it was restricted to lesions in bone and/or BM as documented either by histologic evidence of NB in bone or BM and/or abnormal ^{123}\text{I}-MIBG uptake in skeletal sites. In addition, a minimum of 2 months from last MIBG/PET-CT documented relapse or PD prior to enrollment was required.
L764: ## Dose Selection
L765: As in Trial 12-230, the dose level was 9.0 mg/kg per cycle administered on Days 1, 3, and 5 of each cycle (3.0 mg/kg per infusion) in the presence of s.c. GM-CSF (Days -4 through 5).
L766: ## Trial Treatments, Treatment Assignment, and Blinding
L767: Trial 201 is an open-label, single-arm trial.
L768: As in Trial 12-230, each subject received naxitamab with GM-CSF over a 10-day period in a cycle. A cycle started with 5 days of GM-CSF administered s.c. at 250 \mu\text{g}/\text{m}^2/\text{d} before the start of naxitamab infusions (Day -4 to Day 0) (Figure 8-1). On Days 1 through 5, GM-CSF was administered s.c. at a dose of 500 \mu\text{g}/\text{m}^2/\text{d}.
L769: On Days 1, 3, and 5, naxitamab was infused i.v. over approximately 30 minutes.
L770: ## Dose modification, dose discontinuation
L771: For AEs suspected to be related to naxitamab, management guidance and dose modifications were provided in the protocol. Investigators could discontinue trial drug for adverse events considered related to the trial drug.
L772: ## Administrative structure
L773: Trial oversight was managed by Y-mAbs as the sponsor of the trial. The trial was monitored by a CRO contracted by Y-mAbs. Furthermore, data management oversight and statistical analyses were performed by a CRO.
L774: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L775: All laboratory assessments, including PK and ADA assessments were conducted by an external independent laboratory contracted by Y-mAbs.
L776: In addition, independent efficacy assessments were contracted by Y-mAbs: blinded, external independent reviews of medical images and of pathology slides were conducted by board certified radiologists and board certified pathologists, respectively.
L777: ## Procedures and schedule
L778: Imaging (by CT/MRI and ^{123}\text{I}-MIBG scans/FDG-PET or MIBG.SPECT/CT) and bone marrow pathology (bilateral bone marrow aspirates and bone marrow biopsies) for response evaluation was performed at screening within 4 weeks before treatment, during treatment before Cycle 3, and approximately every 3 months. Complete history, physical exam, blood pressure, hematology, biochemistry, pregnancy test, electrocardiogram, and blood for ADA and PK, were conducted prior to treatment and where relevant during treatment and follow-up. Adverse events were recorded continuously and up to 30 days after the last treatment.
L779: ## Trial Endpoints
L780: The primary efficacy endpoint for the interim analysis was the ORR based on independent, external, expert assessments, up to the interim data cutoff date. As for Trial 12-230, ORR was assessed according to the INRC. The secondary endpoint was DoR.
L781: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L782: A standalone SAP was made for the interim analysis performed to support the BLA. There is only one version of the SAP. The SAP was signed after the statistical analyses of efficacy had started. The principal features in the SAP followed the ones in the protocol. The SAP deviated from the protocol in the following features:
L783: - The definitions of Cohorts 1 and 2 in the SAP were aligned with feedback from FDA and the subsequently amended protocol.
- Analyses of progression-free survival and overall survival were not included (data not yet sufficiently mature).
- The ADA-positive subgroup analyses in the clinical trial protocol were omitted: too few subjects (n=2) were ADA-positive for such analyses to be informative. Instead, subgroup analyses per relevant baseline disease status and disease compartments were introduced.
L784: The analyses performed followed the SAP.
L785: ## Efficacy analysis
L786: The interim analyses provide results for the assessment of the interim primary endpoint, independently assessed ORR, and the secondary endpoint, DoR.
L787: Two cohorts of subjects are enrolled in the trial:
L788: - Cohort 1 includes subjects who are screened negative for ADA and with no preplanned radiotherapy for metastatic lesions.
- Cohort 2 includes subjects who are screened positive for ADA or with preplanned radiotherapy for metastatic lesions.
L789: Primary and secondary interim efficacy analyses were performed on the full analysis set (FAS). The FAS consisted of 22 subjects in Cohort 1 who had evaluable disease at baseline, received at least one infusion of naxitamab and had at least one follow-up before the interim data cutoff date.
L790: Cohort 1 was the only cohort used for primary and secondary efficacy analysis. Cohort 2 (includes one subject only) was included for exploratory efficacy and safety analyses only.
L791: ## Protocol Amendments
L792: The trial protocol was amended 5 times during the period covered by the trial report. None of the implemented changes impacted the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the results. Key features of key amendments are summarized below:
L793: Amendment Number	Effective Date	Change
1.0	Effective date 12 Apr 2018	Specified that the primary endpoint "ORR during the naxitamab treatment period" will be centrally assessed according to INRC.
Added any systemic anticancer therapy within 3 weeks prior to the start of naxitamab to the exclusion criteria.
Updated the long-term follow-up period from 5 years to 3 years.
Updated definition of primary and secondary refractory disease to include disease in bone.

2.0	Effective date 30 Aug 2018	Added the secondary objective "to evaluate the complete response to naxitamab and GM-CSF."
Added the secondary objective "to evaluate the safety of naxitamab + GM-CSF in subjects with positive ADA at trial entry."
Updated secondary endpoint for DoR to define patient response as CR or PR, and to add that data will be censored for ongoing responses.
Updated secondary endpoint for OS, to add that data will be censored at last known date alive.
L794: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L795: Amendment Number	Effective Date	Change
Added secondary endpoint on intravenous opioid use during treatment cycles.
Added secondary endpoint on incidence of AEs and SAEs in subjects with positive ADA at inclusion.
Added the exclusion criteria, "prior treatment with naxitamab."
Added the exclusion criteria, "Karnofsky/Lansky score <50%."
Updated number of subjects planned to be enrolled to include definition of Cohort 1 subjects.
Updated schedule of bone marrow aspirates and biopsies.

3.0	Effective date 24 Sep 2018	Added exclusion criterion "history of anaphylactic reactions CTCAE Grade 4 related to prior GD2 antibody therapy" as part of an urgent safety measure effective immediately.

4.0	Effective date 18 Oct 2018	Added exclusion criteria, "history of anaphylactic reactions CTCAE Grade 4 related to prior GD2 antibody therapy."

5.0	Effective date 07 Feb 2019	Updated clinical management of selected naxitamab adverse reactions.
Updated premedications prior to naxitamab infusions.
L796: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L797: FDA notes that the study population enrolled in Trial 201 as defined above represents a subset of patients with relapsed and refractory high-risk neuroblastoma. Patients with progressive disease at study entry were excluded according to the definition above; this definition has also been applied in the efficacy population for Trial 12-230. Patients with disease outside the bone or bone marrow were excluded per eligibility criteria; in Trial 12-230, patients in Phase II were required to have primary or secondary refractory disease in BM and/or abnormal 123I-MIBG uptake in osteomedullary sites.
L798: FDA notes that while the description of Cohorts 1 and 2 above states that patients in Cohort 2 could have pre-planned radiation to metastatic sites, patients in Cohort 1 could also receive radiation to the primary site of disease provided they had not already received radiation to this site. Patients with primary refractory disease may have discontinued standard first line therapy for high risk neuroblastoma prior to receipt of radiation to the primary disease site.
L799: Although the protocol excluded patients with evaluable disease outside of the bone or bone marrow, several patients had soft tissue lesions present at baseline (see discussion in Section 8.1.4).
L800: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L801: FDA notes that in later versions of the study protocol, the definition of Cohorts 1 and 2 were revised such that patients with bone marrow only disease are included in Cohort 2. The description of Cohorts 1 and 2 provided above reflects the definition applied for the clinical study report and the FDA analyses; therefore, patients with disease confined to the bone marrow are included in this analysis.
L802: Unlike the protocol for Trial 12-230, the protocol for Trial 201 provided extensive advice regarding pre-medication for naxitamab infusion. This was based on the increased frequency and severity of infusion reactions, including serious infusion reactions and anaphylaxis requiring urgent intervention observed in Trial 201 compared to Trial 12-230 (See Section 8.2.4 for discussion). This included (as of protocol version 8.0, dated February 7, 2019):
L803: - Gabapentin, initiated starting on Day -4 before naxitamab infusion
- Opioids (oral preferred)
- Corticosteroids administered 30 minutes to 2 hours prior to infusion, given prior to the first dose and in the following situations:At the first infusion in the following cycle if the subject had a Grade 3 bronchospasm/anaphylaxis at the first infusion of the last treatment cycle
- At the next naxitamab infusion if the subject had a Grade 3 bronchospasm/anaphylaxis at the last infusion
- At the investigator's discretion when infusion related reactions were not controlled by other pre-medications to the attending physician's satisfaction.
- Antipyretics
- Antihistamines
- H2 antagonists
- Antiemetics
- Anxiolytics (if applicable)
L804: ## 8.1.4. Trial Results
L805: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L806: ## The Applicant's Position:
L807: The trial was conducted in accordance with the protocol, applicable regulatory requirements, Internal Council for Harmonisation (ICH) Good Clinical Practice (GCP), and
L808: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L809: with the ethical principles of the Declaration of Helsinki as adopted by the 18th World Medical Association in Helsinki, Finland, in 1964 and subsequent versions.

L826: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L827: ## The Applicant's Position:
L828: A total of 48% of the subjects (12 of 25) had major protocol deviations during the period covered by the trial report. The most common major protocol deviations were pharmacokinetic sampling performed out of window or missed (three subjects), bone marrow biopsies/aspirates performed out of window or missed (two subjects), and deviations to eligibility criteria (two subjects). All other deviations occurred in only one subject each. No deviations affected a subject's eligibility for the FAS.

L856: ## Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L857: ## The Applicant's Position:
L858: ## Treatment compliance
L859: All investigational products were prepared and administered in-clinic. Approximately 97% of
L860: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L861: the actual doses of Naxitamab was equal to the planned dose.
L862: ## Concomitant medications
L863: Treatment, considered necessary for the subject's wellbeing (including supportive care) and not interfering with the assessment of safety and efficacy of the investigational treatment, was allowed at the discretion of the investigator.
L864: A premedication scheme was recommended to mitigate expected AEs, including pain associated with infusion of naxitamab.
L865: Pretreatment before naxitamab infusions included analgesics for pain (e.g., hydromorphone), corticosteroids, antipyretics (acetaminophen), antihistamines (e.g., diphenhydramine or hydroxyzine), and antiemetics (e.g., ondansetron).
L866: During treatment, the following therapies were not allowed:
L867: - Any additional systemic anticancer therapy
- Any local radiotherapy not predefined at baseline
- Any live vaccines
- Intravenous immunoglobulin
- Any investigational medicinal product not defined in the protocol
L868: No major protocol deviations due to the use of prohibited concomitant medications were reported in the trial.
L869: ## Rescue Medication
L870: ## *Radiotherapy to primary site*
L871: For subjects who were enrolled without having previously received external-beam radiotherapy to the primary site, this standard consolidative therapy for high-risk NB should be performed prior to the screening MIBG/PET scans. In circumstances where the subject was in a critical state with an urgent need of systemic treatment from this trial protocol, the subject could receive the standard consolidation radiotherapy to the primary site, after completion of at least one treatment cycle, preferably between Cycle 1 and Cycle 2. The site for external beam radiotherapy to the primary tumor site was to be predefined and recorded in the CRF at baseline.
L872: ## *Radiotherapy to metastatic sites*
L873: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L874: In circumstances where the subject was in a critical state with an urgent need of systemic treatment, the subject was allowed to receive external-beam radiotherapy for sites with bulky/measurable bony metastasis. The radiation should be given after completion of at least one treatment cycle, preferably between Cycle 1 and Cycle 2. The metastatic lesions for external beam radiotherapy was to be predefined and recorded in the CRF at baseline. It was a requirement that the subject had evaluable disease outside the radiation areas to be eligible for the trial.
L875: No major protocol deviations due to the use of rescue medication were reported.
L876: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L877: In Trial 201, the protocol recommended treatment for up to 101 weeks; patients who demonstrate a response were to receive naxitamab cycles every 4 weeks until 5 cycles after demonstration of a response, followed by cycles every 8 weeks. Of the 14 responding patients as of the original data cut-off for the application (June 12, 2019), 7 patients had at least five cycles of treatment after the onset of response, 3 patients discontinued before reaching 5 cycles after response due to adverse events or progressive disease, and 4 patients had treatment ongoing. No patients had received 101 weeks of treatment with naxitamab.
L878: FDA performed an evaluation of the receipt of radiotherapy relative to responses observed in the trial. A total of 6 of 22 (27%) patients received radiation while on treatment with naxitamab: 4 patients received radiotherapy to the primary site of disease (e.g., abdominal tumor bed) prior to demonstrating a response to naxitamab (although per the Applicant, the primary site of disease could not represent a target lesion as soft tissue tumors were not permitted to be primary lesions), 1 patient received radiation after achieving an objective response to naxitamab (patient [REDACTED](b) (6), summarized below) and 1 patient did not demonstrate a response to naxitamab.
L879: - [REDACTED](b) (6): Patient initiated naxitamab in [REDACTED](b) (6), demonstrated CR in [REDACTED](b) (6), and received radiation in [REDACTED](b) (6). The Applicant reported that this patient did not demonstrate disease progression prior to receipt of radiation, but received radiation to the primary tumor because the patient had not yet received radiation typically administered as part of multiagent first-line therapy.
L880: *Reviewer note: The patient appears to have received radiation to a non-target lesion which would not be expected to impact assessment of response.*
L881: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L882: Overall, the reviewer assesses that given the limitation of radiation to non-target lesions, the receipt of radiation is not expected to have contributed to the objective responses observed in the study.

L895: ## **Review Issue: Assessment of Overall Response per INRC**
L896: Individual components of the responses used to generate the INRC assessment (measurement of soft tissue lesions, bone marrow evaluations, and MIBG/FDG evaluations) were independently reviewed and the charter for the independent review was included in the BLA. The assessment of overall response per the INRC was made by the Applicant based on the independently reviewed components. During the review, FDA requested detailed listings including information about the inclusion of soft tissue lesions in the overall assessment of response and reviewed the overall response assessments made by the Applicant.
L897: *Reviewer assessment: The reviewer considered that the overall response per INRC as determined by the Applicant was acceptable in the context of this trial.*
L898: ## **Review Issue: Assessment of Soft Tissue Lesions**
L899: The Applicant provided an overview of response assessment in soft tissue lesions. The Applicant stated that exclusion of patients with soft tissue disease at baseline was at the investigator's discretion, although the eligibility criteria excluded patients with soft tissue disease. The Applicant stated that patients with soft tissue disease expected to be scar tissue were permitted to enroll. Baseline soft tissue disease was followed by CT/MRI and evaluated independently, but the external radiology review was not asked to assess whether disease represented active tumor, and MIBG/PET results have not been considered in the assessment of soft tissue according to the review charter.
L900: *Reviewer note: The INRC assessment of soft tissue disease incorporates use of MIBG/FDG for assessment of response and in defining target lesions. FDA requested additional information including baseline FDG avidity in soft tissue lesions to assess the*

APPEARS THIS WAY ON ORIGINAL
L901: Local radiotherapy was permitted to soft tissue disease present at baseline during the trial, and as soft-tissue responses were not incorporated into the overall response assessment by INRC since these were not considered target lesions, radiation is not expected to contribute to the responses observed. The development of new soft tissue lesions or progression in existing soft
L902: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L903: tissue disease was assessed and, if present, would result in an overall assessment of progressive disease as per the revised INRC.

L909: *Reviewer assessment: If one considers the soft tissue lesion present at baseline to have been active disease, a complete assessment of response per INRC at Week 6 cannot be made; in any case, this patient was considered a non-responder in FDA's analysis due to the lack of confirmed response.*
L910: Patient (b) (6): The patient had a baseline STL (62 mm) which was irradiated on study and the same lesion at EOT (22.3 mm) resulting in a soft tissue PR at EOT. The lesion was reported as MIBG-avid at baseline; at 6 weeks it was read as "the soft tissue focus more to the left, less active," and at week 18 "mild focus." Based on independent imaging and pathology review of the bone and bone marrow, the Applicant considered the subject to have had a CR at Weeks 6, 18 and 26 but PD (bone and bone marrow) at EOT. At EOT, the STL was described as "more active," which is in alignment with the overall response of PD at EOT.
L911: *Reviewer note: Per INRC, overall response of CR requires a CR in all disease compartments; it appears the patient had only a PR (as of end of treatment) in soft tissue. According to the sponsor, the patient received radiation to the primary site of disease after the onset of radiographic response to naxitamab. The sponsor confirmed that Patient (b) (6) would have a response of PR according to INRC but that the assessment of response did not incorporate assessment of the STL. FDA considers that this patient had a response of PR rather than CR, incorporating the response in the soft tissue lesion.*

L915: ## Patients with Soft Tissue Lesions at Time Points after Baseline:

L918: *Reviewer assessment: Based upon the review of the cases summarized above, the clinical reviewer concluded that treatment of STL with radiation is not expected to contribute to the overall responses observed. Ultimately, based on the information provided, it appears the approach of not including soft tissue responses in the INRC assessment for lesions are not assessed as active tumor, is acceptable.*
L919: ## **Review Issue: Duration from Prior Therapy to Baseline Disease Assessment**
L920: FDA considered whether the short time from completion of systemic therapy until the baseline disease assessment could confound the assessment of response to naxitamab. In practice, the urgency to enroll patients, particularly those with primary refractory disease who may have
L921: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L922: demonstrated stable disease, minor response or partial response to prior intensive chemotherapy, onto a clinical trial may render a standard washout period from completion of prior therapy (i.e., at least 4 weeks prior to baseline disease assessment) challenging. Since patients with progressive disease were excluded from the trial, patients with relapsed disease were treated first with other therapy (typically chemotherapy or immunotherapy) prior to enrollment, thus the concern that the effect of prior therapy is not reflected in the baseline disease assessment (and thus that responses observed in the trial cannot wholly be attributed to naxitamab) is greater in this population.
L923: In order to address this review issue, FDA performed a sensitivity analysis excluding patients (non-responders and responders) who had a baseline scan &lt; 2.5 weeks from the time of completion of systemic therapy from the efficacy population. In Trial 201, this criterion applied to 2 responders and 4 non-responders, resulting in 8 responders among 16 patients (ORR 50%, 95% CI: 25.5, 74.5), similar to the ORR observed in the overall efficacy population. Because this sensitivity analysis excluded responding patients with durable complete responses which the review team did not consider reasonably attributable to prior therapy, FDA decided to only exclude those patients who received systemic therapy up until the time of the baseline scan (i.e., those patients with a time from systemic therapy to baseline scan of zero weeks) from the efficacy population; this did not apply to any patients in Trial 201.
