L988: ## 8.1.5. **Retrospective Study 2PR01**
L989: ## The Applicant's Description:
L990: ## Trial Design
L991: Study 2PR01 is a retrospective, noninterventional, noncomparative, single-center, observational study in patients with high-risk neuroblastoma. The patients were treated in a compassionate-use program for named patients. The study was a medical records review, where data were collected from patient medical records and recorded in eCRFs. The objectives were to evaluate the safety and efficacy of naxitamab + GM-CSF and to get an overview of patient characteristics and distribution in the categories of patients treated with naxitamab. The study included 8 categories of patients with neuroblastoma. Categories 1 and 2 (patients with primary refractory disease in bone and/or bone marrow only and patients with relapsed disease with incomplete response to salvage treatment in bone and bone marrow only, respectively) overlap with the subject population in Trials 12-230 and 201. Category 3 (patients with MRD, defined as patients with CR who were MRD positive by qPCR of bone marrow) provide supportive information on the efficacy evaluation of naxitamab. Patients from the remaining categories provide data for the evaluation of the safety of naxitamab.
L992: ## Trial Location
L993: One site in Spain, Europe.
L994: ## Choice of Control Group
L995: Since high-risk neuroblastoma is a rare disease, and due to the retrospective character of the study, there was no control group.
L996: ## Diagnostic Criteria
L997: Not applicable due to the retrospective character study design.
L998: ## Key Inclusion/Exclusion Criteria
L999: To be included in the study, a patient had to meet the following criteria:
L1000: - Have a diagnosis of neuroblastoma as defined by international criteria (i.e., histopathology or bone marrow metastases plus high urine catecholamine levels).
- Have been treated with naxitamab + GM-CSF under compassionate use between 01 June 2017 and 30 November 2018.
L1001: ## Dose Selection
L1002: All patients included in the application were treated after the same dosing regimen as in Trials 12-230 and 201: the dose level was 9.0 mg/kg per cycle, administered on Days 1, 3, and 5 of each cycle (3.0 mg/kg per infusion) in the presence of s.c. GM-CSF (Days -4 through 5).
L1003: ## Study Treatments, Treatment Assignment, and Blinding
L1004: For patient categories 1, 2 and 3, treatment was in accordance with the trials 12-230 and 201, at the discretion of the investigator.
L1005: ## Dose Modification, Dose Discontinuation
L1006: For patient Categories 1, 2, and 3, this was in accordance with the Trials 12-230 and 201, at the discretion of the investigator.
L1007: ## Administrative Structure
L1008: This retrospective study was sponsored by Y-mAbs Therapeutics. Y-mAbs used CROs to monitor study data collected from patient records selected under the protocol criteria, to provide EDC and data management services, and to conduct the statistical analysis.
L1009: ## Procedures and Schedule
L1010: For patient Categories 1, 2, and 3 procedures and schedule were in accordance with the Trials 12-230 and 201, at the discretion of the investigator.
L1011: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1012: ## Study Endpoints
L1013: Patient Categories 1 and 2, pooled: Overall response rate (ORR), defined as the sum of PR and CR, supported by DoR. Investigator-assessed responses were used for response evaluations.
L1014: Patient Category 3: The proportion of patients with MRD at baseline who convert from positive to negative.
L1015: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L1016: A standalone SAP was made for the analyses performed to support the BLA. There is only one version of the SAP. The SAP was signed before the statistical analyses of efficacy started. The principal features in the SAP followed the ones in the protocol. The SAP deviated from the protocol in the following feature:
L1017: The duration of response was not specified as an efficacy endpoint in the protocol and was added as an exploratory endpoint in this SAP.
L1018: The analyses performed followed the SAP.
L1019: ## Efficacy analysis
L1020: Patients could enroll more than once to compassionate-use treatment and could have different disease profiles at different enrollments. However, in the patient Categories 1 and 2, there were 6 unique patients, and in patient Category 3, there were 13 unique patients (i.e., all only enrolled once).
L1021: The full analysis set (FAS) comprised the pooled Categories 1 and 2 encompassing 6 enrollments (6 unique patients). In addition, for Category 3, efficacy data on MRD was evaluated as supportive evidence for the effect of naxitamab. The Category 3 FAS comprised 13 enrollments (13 unique patients).
L1022: ## Safety analysis
L1023: Safety analyses are defined and presented in Section 8.2.
L1024: ## PK analysis
L1025: Not applicable.
L1026: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1027: ## Protocol Amendments
L1028: The study protocol was amended once during the study. None of the implemented changes impacted the integrity of the trial or the interpretation of the results. This amendment added the section on monitoring and quality assurance.
L1029: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1030: As previously stated, FDA does not consider this retrospective study to provide substantive information to support the safety and effectiveness of naxitamab in this application. FDA considers that the retrospective nature of the study may lead to incomplete data collection that renders interpretation of the results of the study difficult. Further, the efficacy results were determined by investigator assessment rather than independent central review, which FDA generally recommends for assessment of ORR in single arm trials to minimize the potential for bias.

L1112: ## **8.1.7. Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1113: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1114: The clinical and statistical review teams conclude that taken together, the data from Study 12-230 and Study 201 provide substantial evidence of the effectiveness of naxitamab, in combination with GM-CSF, in pediatric and adult patients with relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a response of stable disease or better to prior therapy.
L1115: ## **Data to Support Contribution of Effect of GM-CSF**
L1116: The Applicant, at FDA's request, provided rationale to support the contribution of effect of sargramostim (GM-CSF) to the proposed regimen. GM-CSF is currently approved for multiple indications with a recommended dose of 250 mcg/m2/day administered intravenously or subcutaneously; the dose for the indication "BMT [bone marrow transplantation] failure or engraftment delay," suggests a third course of 500 mcg/m2/day can be used for 14 days in the event neutrophil recovery has not occurred with two courses of 250 mcg/m2/day for 14 days. The Applicant cites literature supporting the role of GM-CSF in combination with immune therapy to enhance cell-mediated cytotoxicity, as well as the approval of dinutuximab in combination with IL-2 and GM-CSF in patients with neuroblastoma.
L1117: The Applicant provided results from MSK which tested mu3F8 and GM-CSF in different combinations in vitro, demonstrating that mu3F8 mediated granulocyte antibody-dependent cellular cytotoxicity (ADCC) in a dose-dependent manner with maximal lysis at 10 µg/mL and half-maximal lysis at 0.1 µg/mL. (Kushner and Cheung, 1989) Adding GM-CSF translated to a tenfold or greater reduction in the concentration of mu3F8 required for mediating granulocyte ADCC. Table 8-18, which includes data from the referenced publication, was provided by the Applicant in support of the contribution of effect of GM-CSF.
L1118: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

L1129: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

L1135: ## Subpopulations
L1136: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1137: Because Trial 12-230 is the only trial included in the BLA with sufficient number of enrollments to explore the effect of naxitamab in subgroups, most subpopulations were only analyzed for the PAS population of Trial 12-230. However, response by disease status (primary refractory or incomplete response to salvage therapy) and response in bone or bone marrow were also analyzed for the FAS of Trial 201. Subgroups that were only investigated in Trial 12-230 were by age, baseline body weight, ADA status, and prior therapy with anti-GD2 therapy.
L1138: ## *Response in subgroups by disease compartment and disease status*
L1139: This Section was deleted given revisions to the efficacy analysis and population described above after discussion with the Applicant.
L1140: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1141: In both Trials 12-230 and 12-201, the subgroup previously treated with GD2 had lower ORR compared with the subgroup without prior GD2. FDA conducted exploratory subgroup analyses of the response rate by both prior anti-GD2 antibody exposure and disease status (incomplete response to salvage therapy vs. primary refractory disease) for both studies (see Table 8-8 and Table 8-15). Although the small sample sizes and exploratory nature of the analyses require cautious, results of these analyses appears to
L1142: show a trend toward a better response rate in patients with no prior anti-GD2 exposure, part within the subgroup of patients with relapsed disease.
L1143: ## Additional Efficacy Considerations
L1144: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1145: See FDA Assessment in Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness below.
L1146: ## 8.1.9. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1147: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1148: Table 8-13 summarizes the key efficacy results from the three trials supporting the use of naxitamab for the treatment of high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow in combination with GM-CSF. In the pivotal Trial 12-230, treatment with naxitamab at greater than or equal to 3.0 mg/kg/d (9.0 mg/kg per cycle) led to a durable, statistically significant (p=0.0011) and clinically meaningful ORR of 42.2% in the PAS population, as evaluated by the independent reviewers. The CR rate in the PAS was 31.1%. For enrollments in Trial 12-230 with bone marrow disease at baseline, the CR rate was 80.0% (95% CI: 56.3%, 94.3%).
L1149: The robustness of these results is supported by the consistent benefit of naxitamab treatment on ORR in Trial 201, in Study 2PR01, and across subgroups by baseline disease status (primary refractory disease vs. incomplete response to salvage treatment), disease compartment (presence of bone disease vs. bone marrow disease), age, body weight, prior GD2 therapy, and ADA status. Although the supporting analyses by trial and subgroups were not powered to show statistical significance, it is noted that the majority of analyses met the prespecified limit of 20% for the lower bound of the 95% CI.
L1150: Overall, the enrollment demographics and baseline disease characteristics for Trials 12-230 and 201 and retrospective Study 2PR01 (Categories 1 and 2) were similar. The trial populations analyzed for efficacy were considered to be sufficiently similar to allow comparison of the efficacy results across the trials. In addition, these demographics are sufficiently representative of the anticipated patient population that will receive the commercial product in the United States.
L1151: The subjects included in the primary efficacy analyses received naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF at the planned commercial dose level of 3.0 mg/kg per infusion (9.0 mg/kg per cycle) following the planned commercial regimen of three infusions per cycle on Days 1, 3, and 5 as
L1152: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1153: part of a 4- to 8-week cycle. This dose regimen has been shown in these trials to be effective for the treatment of neuroblastoma in the target patient population.
L1154: Taken together, the efficacy data presented in the BLA demonstrate the clinical benefit of naxitamab for the treatment of high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow in combination with granulocyte-monocyte colony stimulating factor (GM-CSF). Treatment with naxitamab can address the significant unmet medical need, providing effective therapy to a patient population currently lacking adequate treatment options.
L1155: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1156: (Note: Table 13 referenced above in the Applicant's position has been removed)
L1157: The clinical and statistical review teams concluded that the Applicant has provided substantial evidence of the effectiveness of naxitamab, in combination with GM-CSF, in pediatric and adult patients with relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a partial response, minor response, or stable disease, to prior therapy.
L1158: This application is supported by evidence of a substantial, clinically meaningful and ORR and durability of responses in this population, which has no approved therapies, in two single-arm trials. In Trial 201 (n=22), the confirmed ORR in patients with metastatic, relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who demonstrated a response of stable disease, partial response, or minor response to prior therapy per blinded, independent review was 45% (95% CI: 24%, 68%), with 30% of the 10 responders having observed duration of response (DOR) of at least 6 months. The primary efficacy analysis in Trial 201 included 4 patients with prior anti-GD2 treatment; one (25%) of these patients demonstrated a response. In Trial 12-230 (n=38), the confirmed ORR per blinded, independent review according to the revised INRC was 34% (95% CI: 20%, 51%), with 23% of the 13 responders having an observed DOR of 6 months. The primary efficacy analysis in Trial 12-230 included 22 patients with prior anti-GD2 treatment; 4 (18%) of these patients demonstrated a response (95% CI 5%, 40%). Given the limited number of patients with prior anti-GD2 exposure, the exploratory analyses of ORR in this subpopulation should be interpreted with caution; however, the results suggest that naxitamab has clinical activity in patients who received prior anti-GD2 antibody therapy.
L1159: FDA considers ORR of a sufficient magnitude with a meaningful duration of response as an intermediate endpoint likely to predict clinical benefit in this rare population of patients with relapsed or refractory neuroblastoma in bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a partial response, minor response or stable disease to prior therapy. Importantly, although the product was studied and is indicated in patients with neuroblastoma in the bone and bone
L1160: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1161: marrow, assessment of responses was performed according to the revised INRC, which incorporates assessment of progression of soft tissue disease and the appearance of any new soft tissue lesions.
L1162: It should be noted that patients in both trials were permitted to receive radiation in certain circumstances, as detailed in sections 8.1.2 and 8.1.3, although assessment of response excluded sites that received radiation. Patients who received radiation to the only site of evaluable disease, which occurred only in some patients in Trial 12-230, were excluded from the efficacy analysis. Additionally, patients with a best response of progressive disease to prior therapy (i.e., standard therapy for high-risk neuroblastoma, for those patients with primary refractory disease or salvage therapy at relapse) were excluded from Trial 201 and from the efficacy analysis population in Trial 12-230. Therefore, the indication the review team proposes for approval also excludes these patients.
L1163: During the review, the review team considered the impact of a short “wash-out” period between prior systemic therapy and the timing of baseline disease assessments for both studies. FDA considered whether the short time from completion of systemic therapy until the baseline disease assessment could confound the assessment of response to naxitamab. In practice, the urgency to enroll patients, particularly those with primary refractory disease who may have demonstrated stable disease, minor response or partial response to prior intensive chemotherapy, onto a clinical trial may render a standard washout period from prior therapy (i.e., at least 4 weeks prior to baseline disease assessment) challenging and potentially unethical. Since patients with progressive disease were excluded from the trial, patients with relapsed disease were treated first with other therapy (typically chemotherapy or chemotherapy and immunotherapy) prior to enrollment, thus the concern that the effect of prior therapy is not reflected in the baseline disease assessment (and thus that responses observed in the trial cannot wholly be attributed to naxitamab) is greater in this population. FDA performed a sensitivity analysis removing patients with a short interval (&lt; 2.5 weeks) between completion of systemic therapy and baseline disease assessment, which resulted in similar ORRs in comparison to those noted above in both trials, and also resulted in the exclusion of patients with durable complete responses that the review team did not consider reasonably attributable to prior therapy. FDA’s approach was therefore to exclude patients with no washout period between systemic therapy and baseline disease assessments, which applied to one patient in Trial 12-230 as described in the review.
L1164: The difference in ORR between Trials 12-230 and 201 may be explained by a higher proportion of patients with relapsed disease, and a higher proportion of patients with prior anti-GD2 therapy, compared to Trial 201; exploratory subgroup analyses in both trials indicated a lower ORR in patients with refractory disease and in patients with prior anti-GD2 therapy. Though the
L1165: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1166: efficacy population across the two studies is relatively small, the review team considers that, based on sustained responses in heavily pre-treated patients with a high unmet medical need, the application warrants accelerated approval.
L1167: Information on duration of response is limited by several factors. Neither trial systematically captured imaging assessments after discontinuation of study treatment due to reasons other than disease progression. In addition, some patients who demonstrated a complete response were subsequently treated with additional anticancer therapy to consolidate these responses, which is not an uncommon practice in this disease area given the poor prognosis of subsequent relapses. Duration of response was therefore censored prior to demonstration of disease progression in many patients, and the duration of response in the absence of subsequent therapy is unknown.
L1168: Due to small sample sizes and limited follow-up for some patients, there is uncertainty regarding the magnitude and durability of the treatment effect of naxitamab. The review team therefore recommends a postmarketing requirement to verify and confirm the clinical benefit of naxitamab in the indicated patient population by accumulating additional ORR and DOR data across a sufficient number of clinical sites in ongoing Study 201. Postmarketing requirements are described in Section 13.
L1169: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1170: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1171: This safety evaluation is based on data from 273 unique subjects spanning 298 enrollments in four clinical trials and a retrospective study with data collected from subjects treated in a compassionate-use program. Of the 273 unique subjects, 256 subjects were diagnosed with neuroblastoma and 17 subjects were diagnosed with other GD-2-positive malignancies.
L1172: - Trial 12-230 (naxitamab + GM-CSF) includes safety data from 117 subjects, spanning 131 enrollments.
- Trial 201 (naxitamab + GM-CSF) includes safety data from 25 subjects.
- Trial 11-009 (naxitamab) includes safety data from 67 subjects (8 of them with other GD2-positive malignancies).
- Trial 12-116 (naxitamab + interleukin) includes safety data from 12 subjects (9 of them with other GD2-positive malignancies).
- Retrospective Study 2PR01 (naxitamab + GM-CSF) includes safety data from 52 patients, spanning 63 enrollments.
L1173: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1174: All clinical trials are single-arm with no comparator, because of the rarity of the disease and the high-risk population involved.
L1175: The main focus is on safety data for subjects with neuroblastoma exposed to naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF from Trial 12-230 followed by Trial 201 and 2PR01, then further supplemented by pooled safety data from Trial 12-230 and Trial 11-009.
L1176: Data from these trials allow for an informed assessment of the safety profile of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in subjects with high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow. This safety population is also considered appropriate for the detection and characterization of common AEs and to provide guidance on toxicity management.
L1177: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1178: FDA considered the analysis of safety to be supported primarily by patients in Trial 12-230 who received the recommended phase 2 dose of naxitamab (n=72) and Trial 201 (n=25). Analyses were also performed for the overall population of patients in Trial 12-230 (n=117) who received any dose of naxitamab, given that some rare infusion-related adverse reactions such as anaphylaxis may not be dose-dependent. Safety data from studies 11-009, 12-116 and 2PR01 was considered supportive and were not included in the primary safety population for FDA's review of the BLA.
L1179: ## **8.2.1. Safety Review Approach**
L1180: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1181: This application is based on a comprehensive analysis of safety data relevant to the use of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF for the treatment of high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow.
L1182: Monoclonal antibody cancer therapies in general have a number of recognized immune-related adverse effects, such as hives, itching, skin rashes, flu-like symptoms, chills, fever, nausea, vomiting, hypotension, bronchospasm, anaphylaxis, and edema, including angioedema. In addition, therapies with anti-GD2 mAbs (e.g., the two marketed anti-GD2 mAbs, Unituxin and Qarziba [dinutuximab beta]), are known to cause pain, hypertension, peripheral neuropathies, accommodation disorder, and mydriasis.
L1183: As expected with this class of drugs, clinical experience confirmed that the expected toxicities seen with other anti-GD2 mAbs are also the most frequently observed with naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF.
L1184: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1185: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1186: FDA's approach to the safety review included evaluation of original datasets including adverse events, vital signs and concomitant medications in addition to safety narratives. See Section 8.2 above regarding the safety population for FDA analyses.
L1187: ## 8.2.2. **Review of the Safety Database**
L1188: ## **Overall Exposure**
L1189: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1190: The safety population included all subjects who received naxitamab treatment.
L1191: Naxitamab was used in combination with GM-CSF in Trial 12-230, Trial 201, and in 2PR01 patients, as monotherapy in Trial 11-009, and in combination with IL-2 in Trial 12-116.
L1192: Safety data comprised 133 subjects with neuroblastoma who received the recommended naxitamab dose level (3 mg/kg/day for a total dose of 9 mg/kg per cycle) in combination with GM-CSF: 72 subjects in Trial 12-230, 25 subjects in Trial 201, and 36 patients in 2PR01.
L1193: Exposure to naxitamab was considered appropriate to allow for an adequate assessment of safety in subjects who were representative of the intended target population.
L1194: ## **Extent of exposure**
L1195: ## *Trial 12-230*
L1196: Trial 12-230 provides the largest source of data for naxitamab exposure. There were 131 enrollments for 117 subjects and all exposed to naxitamab. The mean (SD) number of naxitamab cycles was 4.8 (3.07), ranging from 1 to 15 cycles. For PAS, the mean (SD) number of cycles was 5.6 (2.73), ranging from 1 to 13 cycles.
L1197: ## *Trial 201*
L1198: In Trial 201, 25 subjects were exposed to naxitamab. The mean (SD) number of treatment cycles was 4.6 (2.46), ranging from 1 to 8 cycles.
L1199: ## *Trial 11-009*
L1200: In Trial 11-009, 67 subjects were exposed to naxitamab at doses ranging from 0.06 to
L1201: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1202: 3.0 mg/kg/day. The mean (SD) number of treatment cycles was 5.0 (4.33), ranging from 2 to 7 cycles. There were 12 subjects in the 2.6- to 3.0-mg/kg dose group, with a mean (SD) number of treatment cycles of 2.8 (2.18), ranging from 1 to 8 cycles. Of these, 5 subjects were in the 3.0-mg/kg/day dose level.
L1203: ## **Trial 12-116**
L1204: In Trial 12-116, 12 subjects were exposed to naxitamab at doses ranging from 0.3 to 1.2 mg/kg/d). Mean (SD) number of treatment cycles were 2.0 (1.21), ranging from 1 to 4 cycles.
L1205: ## **2PR01**
L1206: In 2PR01, overall, 63 enrollments (52 patients) were exposed to naxitamab, with a mean (SD) number of treatment cycles of 3.7 (2.62), ranging from 1 to 11 cycles. Of these 63 enrollments, 36 enrollments received naxitamab (9.0 mg/kg per cycle) in combination with GM-CSF as per the Protocol 201/12-230 dose regimen.
L1207: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1208: FDA generally agrees with the description of exposure for Trials 12-230 and 201 above, and did not confirm exposure for the subsequently listed supportive trials.
L1209: Notably, Trial 12-230 allowed multiple enrollments and the exposure data above reflects data per enrollment. The median number of cycles initiated per enrollment in Trial 12-230 was 4.0 (range 1 – 15, as described above). When considering unique patients enrolled in Trial 12-230 (some of whom were enrolled multiple times), 46% of patients received 5 – 10 cycles, and 17% received 11 or more cycles.
L1210: The median number of cycles initiated in Trial 201, which is an ongoing study, was 4 (range 1 – 8). Fifty-two percent of patients received &lt; 5 cycles and 48% received 5 – 8 cycles as of the data cutoff. In Study 201, among 25 patients who received DANYELZA in combination with GM-CSF, 12% were exposed for 6 months or longer and none were exposed for greater than one year.
L1211: *The reviewer notes that the proposed product label suggests administration of treatment cycles every 4 weeks until CR or PR, then 5 additional cycles. Subsequent optional cycles would be administered every 8 weeks* (b) (4) *Therefore, a patient receiving naxitamab would be expected to receive &gt; 5 cycles of naxitamab, provided they did not discontinue due to progressive disease or adverse event prematurely. Approximately half of the*
L1212: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1213: *patients in Trial 201 did not receive the minimum number of cycles proposed in the product label. Data from Trial 12-230 does provide additional data from patients with longer exposures.*
L1214: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population
L1215: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1216: The clinical development program has evaluated naxitamab in patients ranging from 1 year of age to 34 years of age, with the vast majority of subjects being pediatric patients 12 years of age or younger. Trial subjects were of diverse ethnic backgrounds and represent a range of baseline disease states, with a majority having refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow, thereby providing reassurance regarding the interpretation and the validity of the safety conclusions. Overall, the demographics and baseline disease characteristics of subjects treated in the clinical program reflect the intended target population.

L1221: ## Baseline disease characteristics
L1222: Of the 273 unique subjects in the safety population, 256 subjects were diagnosed with neuroblastoma and 17 subjects were diagnosed with other GD2-positive malignancies (8 in Trial 11-009, 9 in Trial 12-116).
L1223: The majority of subjects treated in the clinical development program were diagnosed with high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow.
L1224: In the pivotal Trial 12-230, 31.3% of the subjects were diagnosed with an incomplete response to salvage therapy prior to enrollment, and 18.3% had primary refractory disease. The remaining subjects in the pivotal Trial 12-230 did not have evaluable disease and received naxitamab as consolidation therapy and were as such not part of the efficacy population but contribute to the safety population. The Trial 201 population includes 64% subjects with primary refractory disease and 36% with an incomplete response to salvage therapy at enrollment. Study 2PR01 included 8 categories of patients with neuroblastoma. Categories 1 and 2 (6 patients) comprised patients with primary refractory disease in bone and/or bone marrow or relapsed disease with incomplete response to salvage treatment in bone and bone marrow.

L1237: ## Adequacy of the safety database:
L1238: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1239: The evaluation of safety is based on data from four clinical trials and a retrospective study with data collected from subjects treated in a compassionate-use program and exposed to at least 1 dose of study treatment. Further details are provided in Section 8.2.
L1240: This population allows for an informed assessment of the safety profile of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF for the treatment of high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow.
L1241: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1242: FDA's evaluation of safety is based primarily on the data from 117 unique patients enrolled in Trial 12-230 and 25 unique patients enrolled in Trial 201. Analyses of data from other studies were considered supportive.
L1243: Given the data collection in Trial 12-230 there are several limitations to the safety database. Comprehensive collection of Grade 1 – 2 AEs was not required beyond Cycle 2 in this study. In addition, action taken with the product in response to an adverse event was not captured in a
L1244: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1245: standard fashion. Dose discontinuations and modifications are described in the review to the extent possible based on analyses of the safety database and safety narratives provided.
L1246: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1247: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1248: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1249: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality and integrity of the submitted data sets and individual case narratives.
L1250: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1251: See description of limitations of the database on the preceding page.
L1252: ## Categorization of Adverse Event
L1253: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1254: The safety evaluation of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF included the following:
L1255: - Treatment-emergent adverse events (TEAEs), defined as any AE with onset date from the day of the first infusion of naxitamab up to and including 30 days after the last naxitamab infusion
- Serious TEAEs, including deaths
- TEAEs leading to treatment discontinuation
- AEs of special interest (AESIs)
- Immunogenicity
- Clinical laboratory results
- Vital signs
- Safety in special groups (by age, body weight, gender, race, prior anti-GD2 treatment, and ADA-status)
L1256: The Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) Version 20.1 was used for coding of AEs in Trials 12-230, 201, 11-009, and 12-116. MedDRA Version 21.1 was used for coding of AEs in 2PR01. MedDRA Version 20.1 was used for coding of AEs for the ISS.
L1257: AEs were graded using the National Cancer Institute CTCAE (Version 4) in all trials.
L1258: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1259: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1260: FDA agrees with the description of adverse event classification above.
L1261: ## **Routine Clinical Tests**
L1262: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1263: In Trials 201, 12-230, 11-009, and 12-116, routine clinical laboratory data were collected and evaluated as described in the protocols.
L1264: Under the 2PR01 retrospective study protocol, routine clinical laboratory data were not collected.
L1265: The summaries included all laboratory assessments collected no later than 30 days after end of naxitamab treatment.
L1266: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1267: Routine clinical tests in Trial 12-230 included:
L1268: - Chemistry including liver function testing (before each cycle and ~3 days after the last dose of naxitamab for each cycle)
- Complete blood count on Day -4 prior to starting GM-CSF, and before GM-CSF on Days 1 – 5.
- ECG after dosing of naxitamab during Cycle 1 and then as clinically indicated
- Evaluation for anti-drug antibodies (ADA) before each cycle
- Tumor evaluation including bone marrow evaluation and imaging (CT, MRI, MIBG scan or PET scan) before cycle 2, after cycle 4, and approximately every 3 months for 18 months from first treatment
- Pain assessment and vital signs prior to and during infusion of naxitamab and prior to discharge from the day hospital
L1269: Routine clinical tests in Trial 201 included:
L1270: - Chemistry including liver function testing on Days 1, 3, and 5 of Cycle 1 and prior to each subsequent cycle.
- Complete blood count on Day -4 prior to starting GM-CSF, and before GM-CSF on Days 1, 3 and 5.Note that per protocol, if Absolute Neutrophil Count (ANC) &gt;20,000/mm3 or platelet count &gt;500,000/mm3, GM-CSF was held until values fall below the abovementioned thresholds. CBC was repeated on sequential days for an
L1271: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1272: ANC &gt;20,000/\text{mm}^3 or platelet count &gt;500,000/\text{mm}^3.
L1273: - ECG during screening, before Cycles 2 and 4 and at the end of treatment
- Evaluation for ADA prior to infusion on Days 1, 3, and 5, in Cycle 1 and prior to naxitamab administration during subsequent cycles.
- Tumor evaluation including bone marrow evaluation and imaging (CT, MRI, MIBG scan or PET scan) approximately every 2 – 3 months with exact schedule as follows:Bilateral bone marrow biopsies and aspirates performed Week 6 (post Cycle 2 (C2)).
- For patients with CR or PR at Week 6 (post C2): repeat sampling Weeks 18 (+1 week), 30 (+1 week), 46 (\pm 1 week), 62 (\pm 1 week) and 78 (\pm 1 week). The repeat sampling planned at Week 18 and 30 had to be scheduled with a minimum of 12 weeks since last sampling.
- For patients with MR or SD at Week 6 (post C2): repeat sampling at Weeks 14 (post C4), 26 (+1 week), 38 (+1 week), 54 (\pm 1 week), and 78 (\pm 1 week). The repeat sampling planned at Weeks 26 and 38 had to be scheduled with a minimum of 12 weeks since last sampling.
- Pain assessment and vital signs prior to and during infusion of naxitamab and prior to discharge from the day hospitalPer protocol vital signs were assessed prior to pre-medication, prior to naxitamab infusion, 30 minutes after naxitamab infusion, prior to each narcotic dose, at completion of naxitamab infusion, at completion of flush, 15 minutes after completion of flush, and every hour while patient is recovering in clinic post-infusion.
- Patients were required to be monitored for at least two hours after completion of treatment, or two hours after the last dose of IV narcotics were administered, whichever was latest.
L1274: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1275: ## Deaths
L1276: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1277: No deaths were reported during naxitamab treatment or in the 30-day period following the end of treatment.
L1278: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1279: FDA agrees with the Applicant's position and has no additional comments.
L1280: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1281: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1282: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1283: ## Serious Adverse Events (SAEs)
L1284: In Trial 12-230, the percentage of subjects reporting at least one SAE was 41.0%. SAEs reported in at least 5% of all SAF subjects included hypotension (11.1%), hypertension (10.3%), pyrexia (9.4%), and device-related infection (6.8%).
L1285: In Trial 201, the percentage of subjects reporting at least one SAE was 32.0%. SAEs reported in at least 5% of all subjects included anaphylactic reaction (12.0%) and pain (8.0%).
L1286: In 2PR01, the percentage of patients reporting at least one SAE was 32.7%. SAEs reported in at least 5% of all patients included angioedema (5.8%).
L1287: ## Treatment-related SAEs
L1288: Treatment-related SAEs reported for Trial 12-230 SAF subjects, Trial 201 subjects, and patients in 2PR01, is presented side-by-side in Table 8-24.
L1289: Treatment-related SAEs were reported for 29.1% of subjects in Trial 12-230 SAF. Treatment-related SAEs reported for 2 or more subjects included hypertension (10.3%), hypotension (7.7%), pyrexia (5.1%), pain (1.7%), pyrexia (1.7%), vomiting (1.7%), anaphylactic reaction (1.7%), and posterior reversible encephalopathy syndrome (PRES) (1.7%).
L1290: In Trial 201, treatment-related SAEs were reported for 20% of subjects. Treatment-related SAEs reported for 2 or more subjects included anaphylactic reaction (12%).
L1291: In 2PR01, 17.3% unique patients reported at least one treatment-related SAE. Treatment-related SAEs reported for 2 or more subjects included bronchospasm (3.8%) and angioedema (5.8%).

L1324: Preferred Term/
Patient ID	Narrative and Reviewer Comments
Mental Status changes	
Patient (b) (6)	This is a 10-year-old male with stage 4 neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who experienced mental status changes two days after completion of 3rd cycle of naxitamab. The patient woke up crying, then fell back asleep and was unarousable according to his mother. He alternated between being unresponsive and being awake and screaming. Upon evaluations the potential etiology was considered to be potentially related to use of cannabidiol or seizures (although no seizure activity was observed on EEG).
L1325: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1326: Preferred Term/
Patient ID	Narrative and Reviewer Comments
He experienced mental status changes after Cycle 4 as well. Again, two days after receipt of naxitamab, the patient was lethargic and difficult to arouse. Upon arrival to urgent care, the patient was 'dusky and unresponsive' and was placed on BiPap. He was treated for UTI, evaluated for seizures and for meningitis/encephalitis. A workup for infectious causes of meningitis and encephalitis was negative.
Reviewer comment: The pattern of two episodes of neurologic changes after receipt of naxitamab is concerning. Of note, this patient had also received the anti-GD2 antibody 8H9 at MSKCC and dinutuximab via a COG protocol. In addition, in response to an information request regarding a subsequent event of cardiac arrest (described later in this section), the investigator stated that this patient had baseline neurologic deficits related to his disease, confounding assessment of this event. Serious neurologic sequelae were not reported for subsequent cycles of naxitamab.

Patient (b) (6)	
This patient enrolled in Study 12-230 (the same patient described in the narrative for (b) (6) above) experienced concomitant Grade 4 hyponatremia, which is the likely etiology of the altered mental status.

Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 24-year-old male with high-risk neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who experienced Grade 3 aphasia following his 7th cycle of naxitamab. Three days following administration of naxitamab he reported difficulty speaking, headache and photophobia; he also reported difficulty spelling out words and texting, and had 2 episodes of emesis. He was evaluated for PRES and treated for hypertension. MRI did not demonstrate PRES and he was treated with gabapentin for pain. The event was considered not related to protocol therapy.
Reviewer comment: The Applicant assessed the event as not related to naxitamab but an alternative etiology is not presented.

Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 6-year-old with high risk neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who developed hypotension and mental status changes after receiving one dose of naxitamab. At the end of infusion she became acutely rigid, postured and was unresponsive and dusky. She was administered Narcan out of concern for hydromorphone overdose and Ativan out of concern for seizure activity with some improvement in responsiveness after medication, and was given fluid due to hypotension (87/49). Workup including brain imaging did
L1327: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1328: Preferred Term/
Patient ID	Narrative and Reviewer Comments
not reveal intracranial disease or bleeding. Both hypotension and altered mental status were considered related to protocol therapy.
Reviewer comment: This event appears to be an infusion reaction with hypotension; the altered mental status may be secondary to pain medication or seizure activity given improvement with treatment with Narcan and lorazepam.

Reversible Posterior Leukoencephalopathy Syndrome (RPLS)/ Posterior Reversible Encephalopathy Syndrome (PRES)
Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 7-year-old with Stage 4 neuroblastoma who experienced Grade 3 PRES two days following completion of naxitamab administration during Cycle 1 of therapy on Study 12-203. One day after naxitamab administration he developed nausea, emesis, headache, and transient bilateral vision loss which was self-resolved. Two days after naxitamab administration, he presented to a local hospital and was found to be hypertensive (150/100). He was treated with antihypertensive medication, but developed a generalized tonic-clonic seizure and was admitted to the pediatric intensive care unit (PICU). He was intubated and EEG demonstrated subclinical status epilepticus for approximately 2 hours. An MRI obtained the next day demonstrated posterior reversible leukoencephalopathy syndrome (RPLS). The patient subsequently received one additional cycle of naxitamab before being removed due to ADA.

Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 5 year old female with Stage 4 neuroblastoma who experienced Grade 3 PRES following Cycle 1 of naxitamab on Study 12-203. Seven days after completing naxitamab she presented to her local hospital with nausea, headaches, abdominal pain, and was found to be hypertensive with altered mental status and seizure activity. She was intubated, treated with antiepileptics and antihypertensive medications. MRI confirmed RPLS/PRES. The patient discontinued naxitamab due to the event.

Apnea
Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 10-year-old male with Stage 4 neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who experienced Grade 3 apnea and hypoxia during his second dose of naxitamab. Episodes of desaturation were self-resolving and occurred while awake and asleep. He was treated with antibiotics based on new right lower lobe opacity on chest x-ray and given oxygen by nasal cannula. Sleep study

L1330: Preferred Term/
Patient ID	Narrative and Reviewer Comments
demonstrated central hypopnea requiring BiPAP while asleep. Patient continued BiPAP overnight while on study.
Patient (b) (6)	
This is an 11-year-old male with Stage 4 neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who experienced Grade 3 apnea and mental status changes following Cycle 1 of naxitamab. He presented the day following completion of naxitamab administration with continued pain as well as hypertension, and was treated for pain and hypertension. Four days after completion of naxitamab he returned to clinic with generalized pain and abdominal pain. His mother also reported that he had been unresponsive for 3 - 4 seconds when she tried to wake him. Upon arrival he had delayed capillary refill and hypotension (60s/30s). During this hospitalization he had brief episodes of apnea. He was treated presumptively for sepsis and evaluated for seizures. The neurologic events were assessed as not related to protocol therapy.
Reviewer comment: The patient's apparent episodes of apnea maybe related to use of morphine for pain.

Acute Kidney Injury
Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 15-year-old female with Stage 4 neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who experienced Grade 4 acute kidney injury following Cycle 1 of treatment with naxitamab. Two days after completion of the last dose of Cycle 1 of naxitamab, the patient experienced nausea and vomiting and was seen in urgent care. Two days later she was evaluated for constipation. Laboratory studies demonstrated a Grade 3 creatinine elevation (4.5 mg/dl; normal 0.6-1.3 mg/dl); her BUN was also increased (40 mg/dl; normal range 6-20 mg/dl). She began dialysis for acute kidney injury. During the admission, she had a kidney biopsy which revealed a mild to moderate diffuse, acute tubular injury, fairly diffuse active interstitial inflammation, and mild glomerulomegaly with increased extracellular matrix. The patient's kidney injury appeared to completely recover by the time of follow-up one month later. The event was considered a dose-limiting toxicity and the patient was removed from protocol therapy. (dose: 2.6 mg/kg/day)
Reviewer comment: The preceding history of nausea and vomiting could indicate that the renal injury is largely pre-renal. Severe volume depletion can precipitate acute tubular necrosis. The interstitial inflammation seen on kidney biopsy could also indicate acute interstitial nephritis, which would most likely be drug-related. The patient recovered following dialysis but as no further naxitamab was given, recovery does not provide additional insight
L1331: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1332: Preferred Term/
Patient ID	Narrative and Reviewer Comments
into the etiology of the kidney injury. This reviewer agrees with the sponsor's assessment that the kidney injury was possibly related to naxitamab.
Respiratory symptoms
(b) (6)	
This is a 5-year-old boy diagnosed with Stage 4 neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who developed Grade 2 respiratory distress during Cycle 6 of naxitamab. He received blow-by oxygen and nebulizers during administration of doses 1 and 2 of naxitamab and was evaluated in an urgent care center the day following Dose 2 due to wheezing and oxygen desaturation with visible work of breathing. He received nebulizers, hydroxyzine, and Singulair. A respiratory viral panel was negative. His 3rd dose of naxitamab for that cycle was held, and he was treated with albuterol, Singulair and Claritin. He was admitted overnight and the event was reported as resolved the next day. He received the subsequent cycle of naxitamab prior to discontinuing due to completion of therapy (the patient demonstrated a complete response).
Reviewer comment: The narrative demonstrates that respiratory supportive care was needed during infusion and subsequently recurred after infusion. The need for respiratory support during infusion suggests that this was an infusion reaction.

Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 4-year-old year-old with Stage 4 neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who developed Grade 3 pneumonia, 12 days after his 1st dose of naxitamab. Later, during his second cycle he presented again with wheezing prior to Dose 2 of naxitamab, and developed a fever post-dose with hypoxia and stridor. He was treated with racemic epinephrine, albuterol, and started on antibiotics for left lower lobe opacity.
Reviewer comment: The event of pneumonia does not appear to be related to naxitamab, but the respiratory distress during the patient's second cycle likely represents a component of bronchospasm related to naxitamab on top of underlying pneumonia.

Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 9-year-old with Stage 4 neuroblastoma enrolled on Trial 12-230 who developed Grade 3 hypoxia during his second dose of Cycle 2

Patient (b) (6)	Gr 2 -8 year old boy diagnosed with Stage 4 neuroblastoma. Patient (b) (6)	This is a 5-year old with Stage 4 neuroblastoma treated on protocol 12-230 who experienced Grade 1 hypotension (serious due to hospitalization. She remained admitted following administration of her first dose of naxitamab (second cycle) through second dose of naxitamab for BP monitoring given the patients' history of PRES with the first cycle of naxitamab. The hospitalization was extended as she was intermittently febrile and hypotensive.
Patient (b) (6)	This is a 5-year-old with Stage 4 neuroblastoma treated on Study 12-230 who experienced fever, tachycardia and hypotension the day following his 3rd dose of cycle 7 of naxitamab. Blood cultures were positive (grew gram positive bacteria in pairs and chains within 12 hours).
Patient (b) (6)	This is a 12-year-old with Stage 4 neuroblastoma treated on Study 12-230 who. The day following her 3rd dose of the 6th cycle of naxitamab, she developed fever and was evaluated in a local ER. She subsequently developed hypotension and was admitted to the PICU. Blood cultures grew Staphylococcus aureus.
Reviewer note: Although fever and hypotension are commonly seen as part of infusion reactions, given the positive blood culture this case of hypotension appears to be related to sepsis.
Patient (b) (6)	This is an 8-year-old boy with Stage 4 neuroblastoma who developed Grade 2 hypotension following first dose of the 8th cycle of naxitamab, patient developed hypotension requiring one NS bolus. The patient was discontinued from protocol therapy per parental decision; the 8th cycle was due to be his last cycle of naxitamab.
Patient (b) (6)	This is a 6-year-old with high risk neuroblastoma who developed hypotension and mental status changes after receiving one dose of naxitamab. At the end of infusion she became acutely rigid, postured and was unresponsive and dusky. She was administered Narcan out of

Patient (b) (6)	
This is a 10-year-old male with Stage 4 neuroblastoma enrolled in Study 12-230 who experienced a cardiac arrest following Cycle 6 dose 1 of naxitamab. Following completion of the infusion and after his last narcotic rescue (approximately 1.5 hours from the time of naxitamab infusion completion and hydromorphone completion), patient fainted and became cyanotic and unresponsive. He received two minutes of CPR and IM epinephrine. He was monitored overnight in the PICU. The event was assessed as not related to protocol therapy.
Reviewer note: In response to an information request the investigator provided rationale for stating the event was not related to naxitamab. This patient had received >5 years of aggressive multimodality therapy including intrathecal and radiation treatments for three relapses in the brain, with residual central and peripheral neurological defects including central and obstructive sleep apnea. He often experienced periods of unresponsiveness apparently unrelated to any oncologic treatment which necessitated emergency transport.
Given the clinical history of this patient, this reviewer agrees that this event may be unrelated to naxitamab; however, as cardiac arrest and sudden death have been rarely reported following administration of dinutuximab, the reviewer feels a contribution of naxitamab cannot be entirely excluded.
L1341: Source: FDA primary clinical review, CSRs for Trial 12-230 and 201
L1342: One additional serious adverse event in a patient receiving naxitamab was submitted to IND 132793 but was not included in the BLA safety population. This patient received naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF according to the MSK study entitled "16-1643: Naxitamab (Hu3F8)/GM-CSF Immunotherapy Plus Isotretinoin for Consolidation of First Remission of Patients with High-Risk Neuroblastoma: A Phase II Study." The narrative provided in the IND safety report is summarized below.
L1343: This is a 13 year-old male with Stage 4 high-risk neuroblastoma initially treated with chemotherapy and surgery. He was then treated with irinotecan and temozolomide in combination with dinutuximab, but he discontinued dinutuximab due to severe
L1344: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1345: neuropathic pain which did not resolve prior to the following cycle. He was later consented to a single patient use protocol with the same regimen as MSK study 16-1643, with doses of naxitamab and GM-CSF identical to those used in Trial 12-230. The patient experienced severe neuropathic pain of the lower extremity after the first two doses of his first cycle of naxitamab. Following the third dose, he experienced urinary retention, lower extremity weakness with inability to walk or bear weight, and severe intermittent back pain. Workup demonstrated T2 hyperintensity of the spinal cord at all levels, and examination demonstrated bilateral lower extremity weakness and decreased sensation, which improved after treatment with IV methylprednisolone. The subsequent day he required increasing respiratory support and heart failure, and he was treated for multifocal pneumonia; he also developed acute renal insufficiency. Naxitamab was withdrawn as a result of the event, and the event of transverse myelitis was assessed as possibly related to naxitamab by the Applicant.
L1346: *Reviewer comment: Given the mechanism of action of naxitamab, and the observation of transverse myelitis in patients receiving other anti-GD2 antibodies, the event seems likely to be related to naxitamab. The additional adverse events of heart failure, pneumonia, and renal insufficiency are not well described in the narrative, which indicates "despite several attempts, no further information provided." The Applicant has included transverse myelitis as a warning in Section 5 of the label, and this reviewer agrees that it should be included.*

L1360: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1361: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1362: Discontinuations due to AEs were few. In Trial 12-230 Phase 1, 4 subjects discontinued due to dose-limiting toxicity (DLT): anaphylactic reaction, acute kidney injury, altered mental status, and hypertension; and 3 subjects discontinued due to AEs: anaphylactic reaction, angioedema, and hypotension. In Trial 12-230 Phase 2, three subjects discontinued due to hypertension, one subject due to PRES, and one subject due to excessive toxicity, where no specific AEs, but a number of nonserious AEs could be linked to the discontinuation. In Trial 201, two subjects discontinued due to anaphylactic reaction, and one subject due to respiratory depression. In 2PR01, four patients discontinued due to AEs: anaphylactic shock, neuralgia, apnoea (nonserious), and tumor haemorrhage (not related to naxitamab).
L1363: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1364: FDA agrees with the description of discontinuations due to adverse events in Trial 201.
L1365: Action taken with naxitamab in response to specific adverse events was not reliably captured during Trial 12-230 and this information was not included as a standard variable in the adverse event dataset. The Applicant conducted a review of narratives and identified adverse events that prompted discontinuation; FDA's manual review of the narratives also yielded 8 patients who discontinued due to excessive toxicity and four patients who experienced an AE leading to discontinuation, totaling 12 patients (10%) in among all patients (n=117) in Trial 12-230 who discontinued naxitamab due to treatment-emergent toxicity as detailed in Table 8-30. This is consistent with the Applicant's description above. Six of these patients (8.3%) received the recommended phase 2 dose of naxitamab and were included in FDA's primary safety analysis for Trial 12-230 (n=72).

L1373: ## Dose Interruption/Reduction Due to Adverse Effects
L1374: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1375: Serious infusion reactions requiring urgent interventions have occurred during naxitamab infusions. These reactions may be mitigated by premedications, supportive medications, infusion interruption, infusion rate reduction, or permanent discontinuation of naxitamab as described in naxitamab product information.
L1376: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1377: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1378: Action taken with naxitamab in response to specific adverse events was not reliably captured during Trial 12-230 and this information was not included as a standard variable in the adverse event dataset. The Applicant conducted a retrospective review to determine events which impaired compliance (in which the entire calculated weight-based dose was not delivered), resulted in delivery of an incomplete cycle, or had dose interruptions or rate changes recorded. Two patients ( [REDACTED] (b) (6) and [REDACTED] (b) (6) ) had a discontinuation or delay due to social reasons (parent withdrew consent after the patient experienced non-serious AEs, and infusion delayed due to the family needing to return home). Nine of 117 patients(8%) had interruptions in naxitamab administration likely attributable to adverse events, though in some cases a corresponding toxicity was not recorded. Table 8-32 lists adverse events leading to treatment interruption. In both trials, the events are almost entirely infusion-related reactions or hypersensitivity events.

L1392: ## Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions
L1393: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1394: ## Treatment-Emergent Adverse Events (TEAEs)
L1395: In Trial 12-230, the percentage of subjects reporting at least one TEAE was 99.1% in SAF and 97.7% in PAS. TEAEs occurring in at least 50% of SAF subjects included pain (98.3%), hypotension (82.9%), abdominal pain (81.2%), urticaria (79.5%), pyrexia (74.4%), vomiting (61.5%), pain in extremity (60.7%), cough (60.7%), pruritus (59.0%), flushing (55.6%), nausea
L1396: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1397: (57.3%), and decreased appetite (52.1%), as well as the laboratory-parameter-associated TEAEs of anaemia (71.8%), lymphocyte count decreased (67.5%), platelet count decreased (65.0%), hypoalbuminaemia (58.1%), hyperglycaemia (53.0%), WBC count decreased (53.0%), and neutrophil count decreased (50.4%). For Trial 11-009 and Trial 12-116, the most common TEAEs reported for subjects were overall similar to those reported for Trial 12-230 SAF subjects.
L1398: In Trial 201, the percentage of subjects reporting at least one TEAE was 100%. TEAEs occurring in at least 50% of subjects included pain (100%), hypotension (100%), urticaria (80.0%), bronchospasm (76.0%), tachycardia (76.0%), pyrexia (72.0%), cough (60.0%), vomiting (60.0%), diarrhoea (56.0%), and nausea (56.0%).
L1399: In 2PR01, the percentage of patients reporting at least one TEAE was 100%. TEAEs occurring in at least 50% of patients included pain (100%), hypotension (86.5%), tachycardia (82.7%), urticaria (69.2%), bronchospasm (57.7%), and vomiting (53.8%).
L1400: ## Adverse Reactions (Treatment-related TEAEs)
L1401: ## Trial 12-230
L1402: In 12-230 SAF subjects, 98.3% reported at least one treatment-related TEAE (see Table 8-33). Treatment-related TEAEs reported for at least 50% of subjects included pain (98.3%), hypotension (82.9%), abdominal pain (80.3%), urticaria (77.8%), pyrexia (71.8%), vomiting (58.1%), pruritus (56.4%), pain in extremity (54.7%), nausea (53.0%), and flushing (53.0%). More than three-fourths of the treatment-related TEAEs reported in the SAF were infusion-related (77.9%). Pain accounted for the largest proportion of infusion-related TEAEs (20.2%) and was reported for almost all Trial 12-230 SAF subjects (98.3%). Other commonly reported infusion-related TEAEs for subjects in SAF group included hypotension (81.2%), abdominal pain (76.1%), urticaria (76.1%), pyrexia (59.8%), vomiting (54.7%), and flushing (53.0%).
L1403: Pain was also reported by other terms such as noncardiac chest pain, facial pain, chest pain, abdominal pain, abdominal pain upper, oral pain, oropharyngeal pain, pain in extremity, back pain, neck pain, musculoskeletal pain, bone pain, musculoskeletal chest pain, pain in jaw, urinary tract pain, penile pain, vulvovaginal pain, genital pain, scrotal pain, testicular pain, ear pain, and lymph node pain. The pool of these terms account for about 28.5% of all treatment-related TEAEs reported for subjects in the SAF.
L1404: When evaluated by a pain score, the highest scores for pain were reported during the 30-minute naxitamab infusion, with pain subsiding before hospital discharge.
L1405: The reported treatment-related TEAEs of peripheral sensory neuropathy, paraesthesia, peripheral motor neuropathy, and neuralgia, were all nonserious Grades 1 or 2.

L1420: ## 2PR01
L1421: In 2PR01, all patients (52) experienced at least one TEAE considered related to naxitamab. All patients had at least one naxitamab-related event of pain in their medical records. Other commonly collected naxitamab-related TEAEs were hypotension (86.5%), tachycardia (82.7%), urticaria (69.2%), bronchospasm (57.7%), and vomiting (50.0%). Pain, hypotension, urticaria, and vomiting were also reported as treatment-related TEAEs for at least 50% of subjects in Trial 12-230 SAF.
L1422: ## Grade 3 and Higher TEAEs
L1423: In Trial 12-230 SAF, the percentage of subjects reporting at least one Grade 3+ TEAE was 78.6%. Grade 3+ TEAEs reported for at least 10% of all subjects in the SAF included stridor (13.7%), hypoxia (11.1%), as well as laboratory-parameter-associated TEAEs of lymphocyte count decreased (40.2%), neutrophil count decreased (27.4%), platelet count decreased (24.8%), anaemia (23.1%), WBC decreased (20.5%), and hypokalaemia (19.7%). For subjects in Trials 11-009 and 12-116, the most common TEAEs reported at Grade 3+ were also reported for a similar percentage as for the subjects in Trial 12-230 SAF.
L1424: In Trial 201, the percentage of subjects reporting at least one Grade 3+ TEAE was 92%. Grade 3+ TEAEs reported by at least 10% of subjects included pain (72.0%), hypotension (60.0%), urticaria (40.0%), bronchospasm (28.0%), depressed level of consciousness (16.0%), anaphylactic reaction (12.0%), and abdominal pain (12.0%).
L1425: In 2PR01, the percentage of patients reporting at least one Grade 3+ TEAE was 100%. Grade 3+ TEAEs reported by at least 10% of patients included pain (96.2%), hypotension (65.4%), urticaria (48.1%), bronchospasm (21.2%), and rash (21.2%).
L1426: ## Grade 3 and higher Adverse Reactions (Treatment-related TEAEs)
L1427: In Trial 12-230 SAF, treatment-related Grade 3+ TEAEs reported for two or more subjects included stridor (13.7%), hypoxia (8.5%), hypotension (5.1%), hypertension (5.1%), urticaria (4.3%), diarrhoea (2.6%), vomiting (1.7%), pyrexia (1.7%), anaphylactic reaction (1.7%), and PRES (1.7%), as well as laboratory-parameter-associated TEAEs of platelet count decreased (16.2%), WBC count decreased (15.4%), alanine aminotransferase increased (3.4%), aspartate aminotransferase increased (2.6%), neutrophil count decreased (1.7%).
L1428: In Trial 201, treatment-related Grade 3+ TEAEs reported for two or more subjects included pain (72.0%), hypotension (60.0%), urticaria (40.0%), bronchospasm (28.0%), abdominal pain (12%), anaphylactic reaction (12%), stridor (8%), and headache (8%).
L1429: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1430: In 2PR01, treatment-related TEAEs reported for two or more patients included pain (96.2%), hypotension (65.4%), urticaria (48.1%), bronchospasm (21.2%), rash (21.2%), hypoxia (7.7%), abdominal pain (7.7%), hypertension (5.8%), angioedema (5.8%), anaphylactic reaction (5.8%), and anaphylactic shock (3.8%).
L1431: ## Conclusion/Summary
L1432: Common treatment-related events were mainly Grades 1 or 2 and included pain (including pain, abdominal pain, and pain in extremity), pyrexia, urticaria, pruritus, nausea, vomiting, hypotension, hypertension, and bronchospasm. More than 75% of treatment-related events were infusion-related. Generally, the highest scores for pain were reported during the 30-minute naxitamab infusion, with pain subsiding before hospital discharge.
L1433: Grade 3+ treatment-related events of importance included anaphylaxis, angioedema, hypotension, bronchospasm, and hypertension. The incidence of treatment-related SAEs across trials was consistent and below 30%. No fatal events were reported.
L1434: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1435: FDA's primary analysis of treatment-emergent adverse events focused on the population of patients who received the recommended phase 2 dose of naxitamab in Trial 12-230 and all patients in Trial 201. In a single arm trial, FDA considers all treatment-emergent adverse events in its analysis and cannot confirm the analysis of treatment relatedness nor the discussion of "adverse reactions" provided by the Applicant above. Tables including all treatment-emergent adverse events were provided by the Applicant below. FDA edited Table 8-35 by removing TEAEs with an incidence of &lt; 10% and laboratory-based TEAEs in the investigations SOC, as these will be evaluated based on the ADLB dataset. In general FDA's analysis of non-laboratory related TEAEs was in agreement with that produced by the sponsor with some minor discrepancies generally consisting of only 1 – 2 patients. FDA added columns which include the calculation based on FDA's assessment, where discrepant.
L1436: The tables below provide analyses by preferred term with no pooling of related terms. For an analysis of adverse events with pooled terms, refer to the discussion of submission-specific safety issues in section 8.2.5 and to the final product labeling.

L1468: ## Laboratory Findings
L1469: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1470: ## Clinical Chemistry
L1471: ## *Renal function*
L1472: Overall, increased levels of serum creatine were infrequently seen. Only four SAEs on renal function were reported (three acute kidney injury and one renal failure). Two SAEs were not related to naxitamab treatment, and in the other two, confounding factors were present: in one, preceding severe nausea and vomiting occurred, and in the other, 3 drugs were co-suspected (naxitamab, GM-CSF, and antibiotics).
L1473: ## *Liver function*
L1474: Abnormal laboratory values associated with liver function were expected in subject populations for the naxitamab trials, given the nature of their disease, prior exposure to anticancer therapy, and wide inclusion/exclusion criteria.
L1475: No significant trends were observed across cycles for clinical chemistry parameters associated with liver function tests and there was no indication of drug-induced liver injury (DILI).
L1476: ## Hematology
L1477: Abnormal hematology values were expected in subject populations for the naxitamab trials, given the nature of their disease, prior exposure to anticancer therapy, wide inclusion/exclusion criteria, allowed transfusions, and bone marrow stimulations prior to inclusion.
L1478: There was no sign of myelotoxicities despite the high number of hematological laboratory TEAEs reported in Trial 12-230. The only observations that seemed to be linked to treatment were the leukocytes and neutrophil counts fluctuating to various degrees during cycles due to GM-CSF treatment. Of SAEs reported across all trials, one febrile neutropenia was related, one SAE neutrophil count decreased related to GM-CSF, two platelet count decreased SAEs were not related, and three SAEs of lymphocyte count decreased were not related.
L1479: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1480: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1481: The following tables were provided by the Applicant upon request and confirmed by FDA. Minor formatting changes were made to the tables submitted by the Applicant. FDA agrees with the presentation of laboratory abnormalities in Table 8-33 with some minor discrepancies (variation by one patient) in incidences of hypokalemia, decreased lymphocytes, AST increased, and ALT increased. The results of FDA's analysis are shown in **Table 8-40**.

L1492: FDA does not agree that significant myelosuppression was not observed in the trial. As demonstrated in the tables above, severe (Grade 3 – 4) thrombocytopenia (40% of patients in Trial 12-230 and 17 % of patients in Trial 201) and neutropenia (46% of patients in Trial 12-230 and 39% of patients in Trial 201) were not uncommon. FDA agrees that the population of patients enrolled in these trials has been heavily pre-treated, but notes that the tables above include patients with worsening laboratory parameters from baseline.
L1493: Based on an analysis performed by the Applicant in Trial 12-230, events of Grade 4 thrombocytopenia lasted 0 – 30 days (median 2 days) and events of Grade 4 neutropenia lasted 0 – 10 days (median 2 days).
L1494: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1495: Some electrolyte perturbations were also observed, with 32% of patients in Trial 12-230 and 8% of patients in Trial 201 experiencing Grade 3 – 4 hypokalemia. Calcium, magnesium and phosphorous were not routinely assessed in Trial 201 and are not included in the table nor in the dataset.
L1496: ## Vital Signs
L1497: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1498: Overall, variations in both diastolic blood pressure and systolic blood pressure around naxitamab dosing days were seen, which was anticipated, considering that hypotension is a known infusion-related reaction.
L1499: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1500: Given the differences in age-based norms for individual patients, individual adverse event data for blood pressure (hypotension, hypertension, etc.) provide greater clinical relevance than average data across patients. See discussion of hypertension and hypotension in Section 8.2.5.
L1501: Two patients in Trial 12-230 and no patients in Trial 201 experienced events of sinus bradycardia that were reported as AEs; events in both patients occurred on the day of infusion and were assessed as definitely related to naxitamab. Forty-six patients (39.3%) experienced sinus tachycardia (1.7% Grade 3) in Trial 12-230. The majority of events occurred on the day of infusion and were assessed as related to naxitamab. Twenty-one (84%) patients experienced events of tachycardia or sinus tachycardia in Trial 201, with one patient (4%) experiencing a Grade 3 event. Almost all events occurred on the day of a naxitamab infusion.
L1502: *Reviewer note: Given the prevalence of pain during infusion of naxitamab, the occurrence of tachycardia may be due to pain. Tachycardia may also represent physiologic response to hypotension, which was also experienced during infusions, or as part of hypersensitivity reaction such as anaphylaxis.*
L1503: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1504: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1505: Abnormal ECG findings were observed in Trial 12-230 and Trial 201. In Trial 201, the clinical significance of ECG findings was recorded. None of the observed abnormalities were considered clinically significant by Investigator.
L1506: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1507: One patient in Trial 201 had an unspecified arrhythmia (Grade 2) other than tachycardia recorded as an adverse event, assessed as related and reported as resolved. FDA requested further information regarding this case, described as occasional bradycardia (heart rate as low as 32 – 38 beats per minute) with fast and spontaneous recovery to &gt; 120 beats per minute). This event appears to be isolated and self-resolving.
L1508: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1509: In Trial 12-230, ECGs were planned to be conducted only during Phase 1; the dataset provided a narrative description or result for abnormal ECGs. Fifty-six abnormal ECGs were identified, primarily describing sinus tachycardia. Patients who experienced prolonged QT or borderline prolonged QT are described below; confounding medications are noted where applicable.

L1514: CTCAE v 4.03 defines Grade 3 electrocardiogram QT corrected interval prolonged as QTc of 501ms or greater on two separate ECGs. Although two patients had measurements of 501 ms or greater, it is not clear that these were confirmed with repeat measurement.
L1515: There were no reported adverse events of QT prolongation. In Trial 201, the datasets indicated that three patients had abnormal ECGs that were not clinically significant. Details regarding these abnormalities were requested by FDA and findings did not include any arrhythmias or QT prolongation. Datasets did not include details such as QT interval as actual ECG measurements were not captured on trial according to the protocol. FDA considered that, due to the low likelihood of direct ion channel interaction, the likelihood of a monoclonal antibody causing QT prolongation is low.

L1517: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1518: No QT trials were conducted.
L1519: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1520: FDA has no additional comments. See description of ECG findings above.
L1521: ## Immunogenicity
L1522: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1523: The incidence of subjects positive for ADA at one or more samples varied from 8.3% in Trial 201 (using a fully validated assay) to 22.1% in 12-230 and 15.4% in PR01. The median time from first
L1524: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1525: infusion to first positive ADA titer was similar in Trials 201 and 12-230 (20.5 and 19.0 days, respectively), ranging from 12 to 64 days.
L1526: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1527: Please see clinical pharmacology assessment of the impact of ADA in Section 6.3.2 and Section 19.4. The assessment is limited by the lack of a fully validated assay in Trial 12-230 and only two patients with positive ADA post-baseline in Trial 201.
L1528: ## 8.2.5. **Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability**
L1529: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1530: See Section 8.1.2, Trial results.
L1531: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1532: Assessments of pain, which occurred in nearly all patients who received naxitamab in the safety population considered for the BLA, were performed in accordance with standard medical practice and did not include any additional patient-reported outcome assessments. Analyses of events of pain are described in Sections 8.2.4 and 8.2.5.
L1533: ## 8.2.6. **Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups**
L1534: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1535: In the population PK model, naxitamab exposure was predicted to increase with body weight. In the safety data available, there were no signs of increasing toxicity with increasing body weight.
L1536: Age was not a statistically significant covariate in the population PK model. In the age Group 2 to less than 6 years; infusion-related TEAEs of pyrexia, hypotension, bronchospasm, and stridor appeared more frequent. In the age Group 2 to less than 12 years, cough and wheezing appeared more frequently; however, children are known to differ in disease presentation by age.
L1537: Safety data do not indicate differences in safety between gender or race.
L1538: For subjects with prior anti-GD2 therapy, there were no differences in TEAEs reported or serious infusion-related reactions between subjects with or without prior anti-GD2 therapy.
L1539: For ADA-positive subjects (Trial 12-230), the relative distribution and frequency of treatment-related TEAEs (including infusion-related TEAEs and Grade 3+ TEAEs) appeared similar between ADA-positive and ADA-negative subjects.
L1540: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1541: The Applicant provided the following tables describing the safety experience by demographic subgroups. FDA confirmed these tables, though numbers differed slightly in the analysis of Trial 12-230. In both trials, it appeared males were more likely to have a Grade 3 or higher TEAE but experienced fewer TEAEs leading to discontinuation. However, given the extremely limited number of patients in the safety population, the differences may be due to chance and extreme caution should be used in interpreting any differences between groups. There did not appear to be significant differences in adverse event trends by age or by race and all analyses are limited by the small number of patients in each of the subgroups.
