L1571: ## 8.2.7. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1572: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1573: AESIs included hypotension and bronchospasm occurring on the same day as a naxitamab infusion and hypertension, and PRES starting at any time.
L1574: ## Bronchospasm
L1575: In Trial 12-230, treatment-related bronchospasm was reported for 19.7% of SAF subjects. All TEAEs of bronchospasm reported for SAF subjects occurred on the same day as a naxitamab infusion. No Grade 3+ TEAEs of bronchospasm were reported for Trial 12-230 subjects, and no TEAEs of bronchospasm were determined to be serious. In Trials 11-009 and 12-116, treatment-related TEAEs of bronchospasm at any grade were reported for a smaller percentage of subjects than for Trial 12-230 SAF subjects. Grade 3+ TEAEs of bronchospasm and serious events of bronchospasm were not reported for any subjects in Trial 11-009 or Trial 12-116.
L1576: In Trial 201, treatment-related TEAEs of bronchospasm was reported for 76.0% of subjects. Treatment-related bronchospasm was reported at Grade 3+ severity for 28.0% of subjects in Trial 201. No TEAEs of bronchospasm reported for Trial 201 subjects were considered serious.
L1577: In 2PR01, treatment-related TEAEs of bronchospasm were captured for 57.7% of 2PR01 patients. Grade 3+ treatment-related TEAEs of bronchospasm were captured for 21.2% of
L1578: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1579: 2PR01 patients. Two treatment-related TEAEs of bronchospasm reported (3.8% of patients) were considered serious.
L1580: ## Hypotension
L1581: In Trial 12-230, treatment-related hypotension was reported for 82.9% of SAF subjects. Infusion-related hypotension was reported for 81.2% of subjects in the SAF. Although TEAEs of hypotension at any grade were frequently reported in Trial 12-230, Grade 3+ and serious events of hypotension were infrequently reported. In Trials 11-009 and 12-116, Grade 3+ TEAEs of hypotension and serious events of hypotension were reported for similar percentages of subjects in Trials 11-009 and 12-116 compared with Trial 12-230 SAF subjects.
L1582: In Trial 201, TEAEs of hypotension were reported for all subjects, and all TEAEs of hypotension were naxitamab related. Grade 3+ treatment-related TEAEs of hypotension were reported for 60.0% of subjects. No SAEs of hypotension were reported for Trial 201 subjects.
L1583: In 2PR01, TEAEs of hypotension were reported for 86.5% of patients, and all but one TEAE of hypotension were naxitamab-related. Grade 3+ TEAEs of hypotension were collected for 65.4% of 2PR01 patients. All of the Grade 3+ TEAEs of hypotension were naxitamab-related. No TEAEs of hypotension reported for 2PR01 patients were considered serious.
L1584: ## Hypertension
L1585: In Trial 12-230, hypertension was reported for 19.7% of SAF subjects. Treatment-related hypertension was reported for 17.1% of SAF subjects. For treatment-related hypertension occurring at the day of naxitamab infusion, 12.0% of SAF subjects reported infusion-related hypertension. Grade 3+ TEAEs of hypertension were infrequent in Trial 12-230, where Grade 3+ TEAEs were reported for 5.1% of SAF subjects and infusion-related TEAEs were reported for 2.6% of SAF subjects. About 30% of TEAEs of hypertension were considered serious. About half of the serious hypertension events started on the day of or 1 calendar day after Cycle 1, Infusion 3, and other serious hypertension started at earlier infusions in Cycle 1. In Trial 11-009 subjects, hypertension was reported for a similar or a slightly smaller percentage than for all subjects in Trial 12-230 SAF. In Trial 12-116, no TEAEs of hypertension were reported.
L1586: In Trial 201, treatment-related TEAEs of hypertension were reported for 44.4% of subjects. Only one Grade 3+ treatment-related TEAE of hypertension was reported for one subject (4.0%) in Trial 201. No TEAEs of hypertension reported for Trial 201 subjects were considered serious.
L1587: In 2PR01, treatment-related TEAEs of hypertension were captured for 28.8% of patients. Grade 3+ treatment-related TEAEs of hypertension were captured for 5.8% of 2PR01 patients.
L1588: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1589: No TEAEs of hypertension reported for 2PR01 patients were considered serious.
L1590: ## Posterior Reversible Encephalopathy Syndrome
L1591: In Trial 12-230, PRES was reported for two Phase 2 subjects. Both TEAEs of PRES were Grade 3 in severity, serious, and related to naxitamab. Neither of the PRES TEAEs was infusion-related, with both events starting 2 to 7 days after Cycle 1, Infusion 3. One subject was discontinued from trial because of PRES, and the other subject continued in the trial. PRES was not reported for Trial 201 subjects, Trial 11-009 subjects, Trial 12-116 subjects, or 2PR01 patients.
L1592: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1593: FDA generally agrees with the discussion above regarding adverse events of special interest. In addition to the events, above, FDA considered neurologic events including pain, neuropathy, and neurological disorders of the eye as events of special interest given the mechanism of action of naxitamab.
L1594: Note that the Applicant uses the term “infusion-related” to indicate events which occurred on the same day as an infusion of naxitamab. In some cases the Applicant has reported “treatment-related” events, whereas FDA considers all treatment-emergent events in its analysis. In cases where the incidence of an adverse event differs in the FDA analysis based on presentation of only related events, or for other reasons, the incidence will be described below. FDA considered anaphylaxis and angioedema, bronchospasm potentially associated with hypoxia, stridor, and hypotension as components of infusion-related reactions or hypersensitivity, which may be clinically indistinguishable.
L1595: ## Infusion-Related Reactions
L1596: Infusion-related reactions were very common in patients in the safety population. The Applicant did not provide a pooled term definition of infusion-related reactions, but described manifestations of infusion reactions separately. FDA performed a pooled term analysis including the following terms: hypotension, bronchospasm, flushing, wheezing, stridor, urticaria, dyspnea, pyrexia, infusion related reaction, face edema, swelling face, orbital edema, periorbital edema, lip edema, lip swelling, mouth edema, tongue edema, swollen tongue, mouth swelling, angioedema, respiratory tract edema, chills, hypoxia, pruritis, rash macular, rash maculo-papular, rash erythematous, rash, and respiratory distress. The analysis was restricted to events which occurred the day of the infusion of naxitamab or the day following an infusion. The intent of the analysis is to capture events within 24 hours; because of the limited number of events across studies which had recorded adverse event start times, the analysis
L1597: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1598: included events which occurred at any time on the day following the infusion. In Trial 12-230, among patients who received the RP2D (n=72), 94% of patients experienced an infusion reaction based on this analysis with 32% experiencing a Grade 3 – 4 infusion reaction; of these there was one Grade 4 event of hypotension (1.4%). Infusion reactions occurred in Cycles 1 – 7 (median cycle 1). Serious events (n=12) included hypotension and pyrexia, and occurred during Cycles 1 – 4. Grade 3 – 4 events (n=34) occurred during Cycles 1 – 7 and 56% of events occurred during Cycle 1. Six patients (8.3%) had an infusion interrupted for an infusion-related reaction based on the retrospective review described earlier in the review.
L1599: In Trial 201, 100% of patients experienced an event meeting this definition and 68% of patients experienced a Grade 3 event; there were no Grade 4 infusion reactions as defined by the pooled term. The incidence of components of infusion-related reactions will be described separately below. Any Grade infusion reaction occurred in cycles 1 – 8 (median cycle 3), and occurred with any dose of naxitamab during a cycle (1 – 3); 48% of events occurred with the first dose of naxitamab of a cycle. Grade 3 events (n=128) occurred during cycles 1 – 7 (median cycle 2). Thirty-three percent of Grade 3 events occurred during cycle 1 and 58% occurred during the first infusion of a cycle. Twenty patients (80%) had an infusion interrupted based on an infusion-related reaction; 80% of patients required an infusion rate decrease for an event of infusion-related reaction. Approximately 50% of events had a the time of onset of infusion reaction recorded. Of these events, the median time to onset was 0.34 hours or 20 minutes following initiation of the infusion (range: 1 hour post initiation of the infusion to 16.5 hours after the infusion). Eighty-five percent of infusion reactions with recorded start and end times started within 30 minutes of initiation of infusion.
L1600: *Reviewer note: Two events were reported with a start time prior to the initiation of infusion of naxitamab, and including pyrexia and urticaria. One is assessed as related to naxitamab. This reviewer suggests that an error in recording start time of either AE or infusion may explain the occurrence of an AE assessed as related to naxitamab occurring before the initiation of the infusion.*
L1601: The Applicant provided a review of serious grouped-term infusion reactions and evaluated for patients who required ICU admission. One patient in Trial 12-230 was reported as requiring vasopressor support for an infusion reaction. Per review of the narratives, several patients required admission to the intensive care unit (PICU) for monitoring. Seven patients with terms meeting this pooled term definition of infusion reactions were admitted to the ICU although only three were assessed as probably related to naxitamab. Other events were suspicious for infection or postoperative hypotension; this reviewer agrees with this assessment based on the narratives.
L1602: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1603: One patient with Grade 4 hypotension required use of vasopressor support; although this event occurred within the time frame evaluated for infusion related reactions, the event occurred the day following the infusion and the narrative suggests that this event was consistent with sepsis, supported by a positive blood culture (*Staphylococcus aureus*).
L1604: According to the Applicant's analysis, 92% of patients with an event of infusion-related reaction (grouped term) required concomitant fluid administration. The analysis in Trial 12-230 was limited by incomplete information on concomitant medications for patients outside the primary efficacy analysis set and included 14.6% of 41 patients who received fluid for a TEAE of infusion-related reaction.
L1605: Pre-medication administered to patients in the safety population was variable. In Trial 12-230, the protocol suggested premedication with analgesics and antihistamines but did not require pre-medication. The protocol for Trial 201 contained specific instructions including the following:
L1606: - Gabapentin and oxycodone for pain, with IV opioids as needed
- Corticosteroids for all patients at the first infusion in the first cycle and in the following circumstances:At the first infusion in the following cycle if the patients had a Grade 3 bronchospasm/anaphylaxis at the last cycles first infusion
- At the following naxitamab infusion if the patients had a Grade 3 bronchospasm/anaphylaxis at the last infusion
- At the investigator's discretion when infusion related reactions are not satisfiable controlled by the other premedications.
- Antipyretics (acetaminophen/paracetamol)
- Antihistamines
- H2 antagonist
- Antiemetics
- Anxiolytics, if applicable
L1607: The requirement for administration of corticosteroids for all patients prior to the first dose was implemented soon before the data cut-off for the NDA and is not reflected in the premedication that was actually administered in the safety analysis set for Trial 201. While 100% of patients received antihistamines and antipyretics, and 91 – 100% received H2 antagonists for all infusions in Cycle 1 (n=23 – 25), only 24% of patients received corticosteroids for infusion 1 of cycle 1. The Applicant stated that the change in the protocol was implemented given the high rate of bronchospasm observed in this trial (76% all Grades, 28% Grade 3 -4).
L1608: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1609: In Trial 12-230, collection of concomitant medication data was limited to patients in the Applicant's efficacy analysis set (n=45); among these patients, only one patient received corticosteroids prior to the first dose in Cycle 1 and only one other corticosteroid pre-medication administration (for infusion 3 of Cycle 2) is recorded for any patient. However, rates of administration of antihistamines, H2 antagonists and antipyretics for all infusions (infusions 1 – 3, Cycles 1 – 13) ranged from 80 – 100%.
L1610: *Reviewer note: Although the safety dataset does not reflect universal administration of corticosteroids in Cycle 1, the reviewer agrees that since this action was taken for safety reasons and given the prevalence and severity of infusion reactions, corticosteroid administration prior to naxitamab should be advised for all patients in Cycle 1 in the product label, as well as in the circumstances outlined in the study protocol.*
L1611: In Trial 201, there were 101 Grade 3 - 4 AEs in the grouped term analysis where IRR. For 31 events, there was no action recorded as being taken with respect to naxitamab, including Grade 3 hypotension (6 patients), bronchospasm, hypoxia, stridor, and respiratory tract edema. However, there were 98 events of Grade 3 or greater AES which did have an action taken, including 60 events of hypotension in 14 patients. Study drug was interrupted in 58/60 events of hypotension which had an action taken with naxitamab.
L1612: *Reviewer note: See discussion of management of hypotension below. This reviewer does not agree with the sponsor's approach to management of hypotension.*
L1613: ## Respiratory Manifestations of Infusion Reactions
L1614: Although there were no events of bronchospasm in Trial 12-230 or Trial 201 which were deemed serious, there were serious events associated with respiratory symptoms related to the infusion, including a patient with Grade 3 hypoxia and hypotension ( [REDACTED](b) (6) ), a patient with pneumonia, dyspnea and hypoxia ( [REDACTED](b) (6) ), and respiratory distress ( [REDACTED](b) (6) ).
L1615: In addition, among patients who received the RP2D of naxitamab, 21% of patients in 12-203 experienced stridor, and 15% of patients had Grade 3 stridor. All but one event occurred on the same day as an infusion of naxitamab. Twenty-two percent of patients experienced bronchospasm and 21% of patients experienced dyspnea. These terms are included in the pooled term for infusion-related reactions described above.
L1616: As described below, the protocol for Trial 201 contained required dose modifications for patients with Grade 2 or greater bronchospasm with hypoxia.
L1617: - Grade 2 bronchospasm: Reduce infusion to 50% of prior rate, which may be resumed if bronchospasm resolves to Grade ≤1.
L1618: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1619: - Grade \ge 3 bronchospasm with hypoxia: pause naxitamab infusion. If patient recovers from hypoxia (bronchospasm Grade \le 2), resume infusion at 50% of infusion rate, and may be increased if bronchospasm Grade \le 1.
L1620: Instructions regarding administration of beta2-adrenergic receptor agonist nebulizers, racemic epinephrine inhalation solution, IV antihistamine and IV corticosteroids were also provided in the protocol but not described in detail in this review.
L1621: The product label includes instructions for dose modifications for infusion reactions as a combined term encompassing bronchospasm, hypotension, etc., and are similar to the instructions from the Trial 201 protocol described above.
L1622: ## Hypotension
L1623: Hypotension is included in the pooled term of infusion-related reactions above. FDA notes that the incidence of Grade \ge 3 hypotension was 7% in Trial 12-230 among patients who received the RP2D, with 11% of patients experiencing an SAE of hypotension. In Trial 201, 60% of patients experienced Grade \ge 3 hypotension. This may be due to the subjectivity of grading hypotension in CTCAE v4 as described below:
L1624: - Grade 2: non-urgent medical intervention indicated
- Grade 3: medical intervention or hospitalization indicated
- Grade 4: life-threatening and urgent intervention indicated.
L1625: The toxicity Grade assigned depends upon the healthcare provider's definition of "non-urgent;" this reviewer would argue that infusion-associated hypotension would typically prompt urgent medical intervention (such as stopping or slowing an infusion or providing fluid support if needed). Therefore the difference in incidence of Grade \ge 3 hypotension between the two primary studies may not represent a true difference in incidence but rather differing interpretation of the grading system.
L1626: (b) (4)
L1627: As indicated in Section 11,
L1628: FDA revised the product label to include a Warning for serious infusion-related reactions, which includes hypotension.
L1629: The protocol for Trial 201 included instructions for management of hypotension. Infusion interruption or infusion rate reduction was not required for Grade 2 hypotension. Instructions are summarized below.
L1630: - Grade 3 hypotension without other symptoms:Decrease in the rate of naxitamab by 50%; if patient stabilizes, consider resuming to normal infusion speed based on clinical judgement
- Grade 3 hypotension with other symptoms such as bradycardia, hypoxia, 'not adequately
L1631: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1632: responding' or Grade hypotension:
L1633: - Pause naxitamab infusion. If patient warm and well-perfused, treat hypotension, may resume naxitamab infusion at 50% of prior rate upon resolution to Grade \le 2.
- If patient not warm and well-perfused, inadequate response to fluid resuscitation, consider naloxone, epinephrine IV/IM. May resume naxitamab infusion at 50% of prior rate upon resolution to Grade \le 2.
- If patient continues to be hypotensive/difficult to arouse or Grade 3 hypotension recurs, stop naxitamab infusion.
- Subsequent infusions initiated at discretion of investigator at 50% of rate of infusion prior to the event, and investigator should consider adjustments of pre-medication.
L1634: (b) (4)
L1635: Reviewer note: In the opinion of this reviewer,
L1636: is not adequately conservative. FDA has proposed a management strategy for interruption or rate reduction of naxitamab in the event of infusion-related reactions as a group
L1637: (b) (4)
L1638: and proposes to require rate reduction in patients with Grade 2 IRRs, with interruption in patients with Grade 3 IRRs and permanent discontinuation in patients with Grade 4 IRRs or Grade 3 IRRs that do not respond to intervention (see naxitamab product label for details).
L1639: ## Anaphylaxis and Angioedema
L1640: Three patients on Trial 201 (12%) and two patients enrolled on Trial 12-203 at any dose (n=117) experienced serious events of anaphylaxis. One event in Trial 201 was attributed to GM-CSF. A challenge in interpretation of infusion related reactions is that infusion reactions with monoclonal antibodies may overlap with anaphylaxis and may be indistinguishable. (LaCasce, 2020)
L1641: The majority of events of anaphylaxis (excluding the event attributed to GM-CSF) resulted in discontinuation of naxitamab. However, in response to an event of Grade 3 anaphylaxis in Study 201 (patient (b) (6)), naxitamab was not discontinued. The patient received the remainder of the first cycle of naxitamab, but experienced Grade 4 anaphylaxis during the first dose of naxitamab during cycle 2.
L1642: This reviewer notes that CTCAE v 4.0 defines Grade 3 and 4 anaphylaxis as follows:
L1643: - Grade 3: Symptomatic bronchospasm, with or without urticaria; parenteral intervention indicated; allergy-related edema/angioedema; hypotension
L1644: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1645: - *Grade 4: Life-threatening consequences; urgent intervention indicated*
The reviewer notes that untreated anaphylaxis of any Grade is generally life-threatening and thus there is potential that different providers would Grade the same event differently. This reviewer considers that all anaphylactic reactions should prompt permanent discontinuation of naxitamab given the serious risk of life-threatening reactions, (b) (4)
L1646: The case of patient (b) (6) (Study 201) also illustrates the potential need for extended monitoring following infusion for patients treated for infusion-related reactions. This patient experienced the event of Grade 3 anaphylaxis previously referenced three hours after infusion. He had received diphenhydramine for lip and tongue swelling at the end of infusion. (b) (4)
L1647: hence, FDA recommended that product labeling include instructions for close monitoring for signs and symptoms of an infusion reaction during and for at least 2 hours following completion of the naxitamab infusion. Narratives from Study 12-230 do not all include the exact time of onset of signs or symptoms associated with infusion reactions or anaphylaxis, whereas narratives from Study 201 collected this information in approximately 50% of cases of infusion reactions. Onset of hypotension or bronchospasm was generally during the infusion or at the end of the infusion. Since the majority of the safety data is derived from Study 12-230, an assessment of the exact timing of onset of infusion reactions and anaphylaxis is limited.
L1648: ## Hypertension
L1649: FDA agrees with the description of the incidence of hypertension above. In Trial 12-230, there were 12 SAEs of hypertension (10.2%), and four led to discontinuation of naxitamab. In addition, two of these (which led to discontinuation) had MRI findings potentially consistent with PRES, as described below. Among patients who received the RP2D of naxitamab (n=72), 10 (14%) had an SAE of hypertension.
L1650: This reviewer notes that there are limitations to the CTCAE v 4.0 grading of hypertension for children; CTCAE v 5.0 has revised the definition for pediatric patients. Normal blood pressure for children varies and norms are classified based on gender, height percentile, and age. CTCAE v 4.0 provides the following guidance for grading hypertension in children:
L1651: - Grade 1: No specific mention of children; for adults, prehypertension (systolic BP 120 - 139 mm Hg or diastolic BP 80 - 89 mm Hg)
- Grade 2: Recurrent or persistent (&gt;=24 hrs) BP &gt;ULN; monotherapy indicated
L1652: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1653: - Grade 3: Stage 2 hypertension (systolic BP \ge160 mm Hg or diastolic BP \ge100 mm Hg); medical intervention indicated; more than one drug or more intensive therapy than previously used indicated. Pediatric: same as adult.
- Grade 4: Life-threatening consequences (e.g., malignant hypertension, transient or permanent neurologic deficit, hypertensive crisis); urgent intervention indicated. Pediatric: Same as adult.
L1654: In practice, for children &lt; 13 years of age, Stage 2 hypertension is classified as systolic and diastolic blood pressures \ge95th percentiles + 12mm Hg, or \ge 140/90mm Hg (whichever is lower), and for children 13 years and older, this is defined as \ge 140/90mm Hg. Therefore this reviewer considers that the severity of some events captured as "Grade 2" in patients who had systolic blood pressures between 140 - 160 mm Hg underestimates the severity of hypertension when considering the patient's age-based norms. This should be considered when interpreting the data describing this toxicity.
L1655: Two events of hypertension not reported as PRES in Trial 12-230 had MRI findings potentially consistent with PRES, as described in the narratives below:
L1656: Patient (b) (6): This is a 3 year old with stage 4 neuroblastoma who experienced Grade 2 hypertension and Grade 1 urinary retention during his first cycle of naxitamab (3.0 mg/kg/dose). He received naxitamab as an inpatient for social reasons. He had intermittent hypertension starting after his first dose (145/96) which continued past the administration of the third dose of naxitamab. An MRI done two days after the administration of the last dose of naxitamab showed 'diffuse white matter lesions that could be suggestive of PRES.' He was discharged 10 days following completion of naxitamab. Naxitamab was discontinued because of the event.
L1657: Patient (b) (6): This is a three year old boy originally diagnosed with Stage 3 neuroblastoma who experienced Grade 3 hypertension the day following infusion of naxitamab (3.0 mg/kg/dose). He was evaluated in urgent care for a routine blood pressure check and had a BP of 130/88 while asleep (99th percentile for age 112/71). He had been experiencing nausea at home but had no neurologic changes concerning for PRES. He was admitted for blood pressure and nausea control and was discharged the following day, but follow-up the subsequent day (3 days post infusion) demonstrated worsened hypertension (199/78). The family was given a prescription for enalapril to be given as needed. During his second cycle of naxitamab, he experienced a serious AE of fever and hypertension. One day after his third dose of naxitamab he was admitted due to persistent fever and was hypertensive on exam. MRI showed suspicion for subtle early PRES. Naxitamab was discontinued due to the event.
L1658: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1659: For the 7 events of hypertension for which a start time was recorded in Trial 201, the median time to onset was 1.4 hours (range 0.4 to 3.9 hours) and median duration was 2.2 hours (range 0.5 hours to 7 days). Events of hypertension were more likely to be recorded on the 3rd infusion of naxitamab of a cycle. For all events of hypertension (regardless of whether an exact start time was recorded) in Trial 201 (n=25), events occurred on a median of day 1, with day 1 being the day of the infusion (range 1 – 2) relative to an infusion of naxitamab.
L1660: In Trial 12-230, event start time was rarely recorded and the analysis was conducted in days. Events occurred from days 1 – 9 relative to an infusion of naxitamab (median day 1) and the majority of events occurred the day of or the day following an infusion (31/40, or 78%). The median duration in Trial 12-230 was 1 day (range 1 to 29 days). Among Grade 3 events, the median duration was 3.0 days (range 1 to 5 days).
L1661: *Reviewer note: The timing of onset of hypertension appears to have a wider range in Trial 12-230. The reviewer hypothesizes that greater awareness of this adverse reaction may have led to additional monitoring and earlier diagnosis; however, it may also be a result of the small safety database for Trial 201.*
L1662: Eight patients (11%) in the safety population in Trial 12-230 and 7 patients (25%) in Trial 201 received antihypertensive medications.
L1663: ## Neurotoxicity including Pain, Neuropathy and Neurological Disorders of the Eye
L1664: ## Pain
L1665: Pain was present in nearly all patients in the safety populations of both Trials 201 and 12-230. In Trial 201, 100% of patients had any Grade of pain and 72% of patients had Grade \ge 3 pain. Events of pain lasted 0 – 28 days with a median of 0 days (indicating the event resolved the same day). There were 345 events in total, and all but 4 occurred on the day of an infusion of naxitamab. Among events that had a recorded start time in Trial 201, the median time to onset was 0.18 hours or approximately 11 minutes (range 0 to 22 hours) following initiation of the infusion; for Grade 3 events the median time to onset was also 0.18 hours or 11 minutes (range 0.05 hours to 1 hour). Events of pain lasted a median of 2.3 hours (range 0.08 to 249 hours or 10 days). Among Grade 3 events, the median duration was 2.2 hours (range 0.08 to 60 hours or 2.5 days).
L1666: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1667: In Trial 12-230, 94% of patients treated at the recommended phase 2 dose had any Grade of pain and no patients had an event of Grade 3 or greater pain. Events of pain lasted 0 – 29 days with a median of 0 days. There were 395 events total and 23 did not occur on the day of an infusion, occurring up to 18 days following infusion.
L1668: A grouped term analysis of pain included the following terms: pain, abdominal pain, pain in extremity, bone pain, neck pain, back pain, non-cardiac chest pain, flank pain, and musculoskeletal pain. In Trial 201, 100% of patients had any Grade of pain and 72% of patients had Grade \ge 3 pain. Based on an analysis of all events performed in days, events of pain lasted 0 – 62 days with a median of 0 days (indicating the event resolved the same day). One patient in Trial 201 had a dose interruption and one had a dose decrease due to an event of pain; upon clarification from the study site, the dose of naxitamab was not decreased, but the patient was given medication for pain.
L1669: Based on an analysis of the subset of events with recorded start and end times, grouped term events of pain lasted a median of 1.9 hours (range 5 minutes – 2 days).
L1670: In Trial 12-230, 94% of patients treated at the recommended phase 2 dose had any Grade of pain and 2.8% of patients had an event of Grade 3 or greater pain. Events of pain lasted 0 – 29 days with a median of 0 days.
L1671: Trial 201 advised administration of gabapentin starting on Day -4 of each cycle and continuing through Day 7. Ninety-two percent (92%) of patients received gabapentin premedication in Trial 201. Both study protocols advised premedication with opioids prior to administration of naxitamab. The protocol for Trial 201 advises administration of oxycodone 45 – 60 minutes prior to naxitamab.
L1672: *Reviewer note: Given the prevalence of pain with naxitamab infusion, the product label will include guidance on pre-medication for pain.*
L1673: ## Neurological Disorders of the Eye
L1674: FDA performed a pooled term analysis to evaluate neurological disorders of the eye. The pooled term "eye disorders" excluded events with terms related to infection, swelling, hemorrhage and could be related to neurologic disorders of the eye and included the terms photophobia, pupils unequal, vision blurred, accommodation disorder, optic nerve disorder, mydriasis, and visual impairment.
L1675: In Trial 12-330, among the 117 patients treated with naxitamab at any dose, there were 32 events of neurological disorders of the eye in 19 patients (16%). Seventeen events were
L1676: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1677: assessed as definitely, possibly, or probably related to naxitamab. There were no serious events and all were Grade 1 - 2. All events resolved (resolution occurred on a median of day 1). In the subset of patients who received the RP2D (n=72), 19% of patients experienced event meeting on the terms listed above; there were no Grade 3 - 4 events. Events occurred from days 1 - 29 relative to an infusion (median day 1, or the day of infusion). All events resolved and lasted 0 - 21 days (median 1 day).
L1678: In Trial 201, 24% of patients had an event in the pooled term analysis (including terms mydriasis, pupils unequal, vision blurred). Events occurred a range of 1 - 18 days relative to most recent infusion (median 1 day) during cycles 1 - 4 (median cycle 2). Two of 9 events (mydriasis) were ongoing at data cut-off; of events that resolved, the range of duration of events was 0 - 84 days with median of 17 days. All were grades 1 - 2 and none were serious. The Applicant provided follow-up information regarding the ongoing cases of Grade 2 mydriasis; these two cases were not resolved as of the data cut-off for the 120-day safety update with reported symptoms of photophobia. In addition, two other cases of mydriasis with onset after the DCO for the original application were provided; one of these events was reported as ongoing. The ongoing events have durations of approximately 17 months, 6 months, and 6 weeks.
L1679: This reviewer notes that mydriasis does not have a dedicated CTCAE term according to v 4.03, but Grade 2 eye disorders are defined as follows: "Moderate; minimal, local or noninvasive intervention indicated; limiting age-appropriate instrumental ADL."
L1680: Based on the prolonged nature of these events of mydriasis with accompanying symptoms, FDA considered that intervention with respect to naxitamab administration may be indicated in some instances including those with visual impairment. The final recommendations are reflected in Section 2 of the product label.
L1681: ## Neuropathy
L1682: A grouped term analysis was performed for neuropathy including the following terms: peripheral sensory neuropathy, peripheral motor neuropathy, paresthesia, neuralgia. In Trial 12-230, 25% of 72 patients who received the RP2D experienced an event. Seventeen of 34 (50%) events occurred on the day of an infusion and all but two events occurred within 1 week of infusion (range 1 - 25 days, median 1.5). Events lasted less than one day to 22 days with a median duration of median less than one day (the event resolved later the same day). All events were Grade 1 - 2 and there were no serious events.
L1683: In Trial 201, 32% of patients had an event of neuropathy as defined by the grouped term analysis with terms listed above. Events occurred on a median of day 1 relative to the infusion
L1684: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1685: (the day of infusion), with a range of 1 - 17 days from the infusion. Events occurred during cycles 1 – 5 (median cycle 2). Three events were not resolved per the dataset; a request for follow-up data from the sponsor indicated that two of these events had resolved, with the remaining event of Grade 1 peripheral neuropathy listed as ongoing (event duration as of August 2020 was approximately 16 months). For those that resolved, median duration was 5.5 days (range 0 - 22 days). All events were Grade 1 – 2 and there were no serious events.
L1686: The study protocol for Trial 201 required discontinuation of naxitamab in the instance of Grade 4 sensory neuropathy or Grade 3 sensory neuropathy that interferes with daily activities for more than 2 weeks. FDA considered that a lower threshold would be indicated for discontinuation in the event of motor neuropathy given the interference with daily activities. The final recommendations are reflected in Section 2 of the product label.
L1687: ## Urinary Retention
L1688: Urinary retention was investigated as a potential safety signal. Among patients treated on Trial 12-230 at any dose of naxitamab, three patients had serious events of urinary retention or urine output decreased. One serious event was deemed unlikely related to naxitamab by this reviewer due to potential obstruction with ureteral stent. Two cases appeared potentially related though confounded by 1) baseline urinary incontinence and "disease-related neurologic defects" and 2) concomitant medication (sedation, opioids). The second case is discussed below:
L1689: - Patient (b) (6) experienced a serious adverse event of Grade 1 urinary retention which lasted 10 days. The patient was sedated for bone marrows and CT and MIBG scans on the day the event started, and the event resolved 5 days after last administration of opioids.
L1690: There were two non-serious cases of prolonged urinary retention in Trial 12-230 which occurred in a single patient.
L1691: - Patient (b) (6) had two events of urinary retention lasting 5 and 24 days. The event lasting 5 days in duration resolved on the third day after opioids were discontinued and did not require hospitalization. The event recorded as lasting 24 days had no source documentation available in the electronic medical record and thus a narrative could not be provided.
L1692: In Trial 12-230 among patients treated at any dose, 8 patients (6.8%) had 11 events of urinary retention, with no Grade 3 – 4 events. The events occurred on days 1 - 6 relative to the most recent infusion of naxitamab, with a median of 1 day. All events were reported as resolved with
L1693: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1694: a duration 0 - 24 days (median 3 days). In 12-230 among patients who received the RP2D, 3 patients experienced 5 events of urinary retention. All events occurred on the day of infusion and all but one event occurred during cycle 1 (remaining event during cycle 2). All events resolved, and lasted 0 - 24 days (median 5 days). One event was serious. All events were Grade 1 - 2. In Trial 201, 1 patient had 3 events of urinary retention. All occurred on the same day as an infusion and resolved the same day.
L1695: FDA considered that events of urinary retention that do not resolve for a prolonged period after discontinuation of opioids should prompt discontinuation of naxitamab. The final recommendations are described in Section 2 of the product label.

L1697: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1698: Not applicable.
L1699: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1700: FDA agrees this section is not applicable.

L1736: ## **Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting**
L1737: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1738: Potential safety concerns beyond the risks conveyed in the proposed labeling are not expected. Routine pharmacovigilance will be conducted to monitor the safety of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF.
L1739: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1740: Based on the occurrence of severe infusion reactions, FDA determined that a black box warning was necessary to advise healthcare providers of this risk and need for careful monitoring and management. The safety experience with this product represented in this application is largely derived from a single center. Additional safety data to characterize the risk of severe infusion reactions in a multicenter setting will be requested from the ongoing multicenter trial (201) as a postmarketing requirement.
L1741: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1742: ## 8.2.11. **Integrated Assessment of Safety**
L1743: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1744: The assessment of safety is supported by data from four clinical trials and data collected for patients treated under compassionate use.
L1745: Common treatment-related events were mainly Grades 1 or 2 and included pain, abdominal pain, pain in extremity, pyrexia, urticaria, pruritus, nausea, vomiting, hypotension, hypertension, and bronchospasm. More than 75% of treatment-related events were infusion related.
L1746: Grade 3+ treatment-related events of importance included anaphylaxis, angioedema, hypotension, bronchospasm, and hypertension. The incidence of treatment-related SAEs across trials was comparable and below 30%. No deaths occurred.
L1747: On the basis of its mechanism of action, naxitamab may cause severe pain, which is expected to occur for many patients receiving naxitamab. Pain was the most frequently reported adverse reaction, reported by almost all subjects. These events were associated with naxitamab infusion and resolved quickly after the infusion was complete. In the naxitamab clinical program, pain was managed by premedication. Gabapentin was administered at home starting 3 days before the day of naxitamab infusion. On the day of infusion, opioids were given before and during infusion to manage pain. Generally, the highest scores for pain were reported during the 30-minute naxitamab infusion, with pain subsiding before hospital discharge.
L1748: ## *Infusion reactions*
L1749: Naxitamab has been shown to cause infusion reactions, including anaphylaxis, bronchospasm, skin reactions, oedema, and hypotension. Infusion reactions generally occurred soon after initiating naxitamab and may last for up to 2 hours after the infusion. Infusion reactions are most frequent during first infusion in each cycle. The onset of hypotension and bronchospasm generally occurred during naxitamab infusion. Hypotension and bronchospasm tend to be more frequent and more severe at the first infusion of a cycle and worst at the first infusion of the first cycle. In the clinical development program, subjects were premedicated with antihistamines, antipyretics, and corticosteroids to minimize these risks. Furthermore, mitigations were in place, including reduction of infusion speed and ability to give concomitant medications, such as nebulizer with beta2-adrenergic receptor agonist; S2 inhalation; and additional antihistamines, antipyretics, and/or corticosteroids.
L1750: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1751: Anaphylaxis can be a serious reaction to any monoclonal antibody therapy. To mitigate the risk of anaphylaxis, administration of naxitamab will be contraindicated for any patient that has had an anaphylactic reaction (CTCAE Grade 4) to another anti-GD2 therapeutic. Part of the premedication regimen for naxitamab is the administration of corticosteroids and antihistamines to aid in the prevention of severe immune reactions, and guidance is provided to physicians to aid in the management of anaphylactic reactions in the product information. Naxitamab can be administered in an out-patient setting, but must be administered in a facility by a health care professional with immediate access to emergency equipment and appropriate medical support to manage severe adverse reactions, if they occur.
L1752: ## *Hypertension*
L1753: Naxitamab may cause severe hypertension. Not all hypertension started on a day of a naxitamab infusion, but occurred later. For treatment-related hypertension occurring at the day of naxitamab infusion, 12.0% of SAF subjects reported infusion-related hypertension. In Trial 12-230, about half of the serious hypertensions started on the day of or 1 calendar day after Cycle 1, Infusion 3, and other serious hypertension events started at earlier infusions in Cycle 1. PRES was observed in two subjects, but only one subject discontinued treatment. Mitigations for hypertension are defined in the label, including additional BP measurements, (b) (4)
L1754: ## *Subpopulations*
L1755: No safety concerns were raised based on subgroup analyses. Safety data demonstrated patterns of events consistent with those reported for the overall population.
L1756: ## *Laboratory findings*
L1757: In the naxitamab clinical development program, there were no signs of nephrotoxicity, drug-induced liver injury, or myelosuppression.
L1758: In conclusion, the safety profile of naxitamab was well characterized in the clinical development program of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF for patients with high-risk refractory or relapsed neuroblastoma in bone and/or bone marrow.
L1759: Events of pain, hypotension, bronchospasm, and hypertension (including CTCAE Grade 3 and above) are expected to occur for many patients receiving naxitamab, based on the clinical data. To minimize or mitigate these events, guidance for the management of these is provided in the label.
L1760: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1761: Naxitamab treatment has proved tolerable for the majority of subjects as shown by the relatively small number of withdrawals because of adverse events. Most adverse reactions are limited to the day of infusion, and the effects of the treatment have been manageable through premedication regimens, analgesic administration during infusions, and careful monitoring of patients during and in the period after infusion.
L1762: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1763: The safety of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF was evaluated in a subpopulation of patients with high-risk neuroblastoma in bone or bone marrow and had relapsed or refractory disease, or were in complete remission, from two open-label, single arm studies, Study 201 (n=25) and Study 12-230 (n=72). In both studies, patients received naxitamab as three separate intravenous infusions of 3 mg/kg (day 1,3 and 5) in first week of a pre-planned 4-week cycle. Patients also received GM-CSF subcutaneously at 250 \mug/m2/day on days -4 to 0 and at 500 \mug/m2/day on Days 1 to 5.
L1764: During Trials 201 and 12-230 as of the data cut-off for the application, there were no deaths. Cumulative safety data indicated that SAEs occurred in 32% of patients in Trial 201 and 40% of patients in Trial 12-230. Serious adverse reactions in &gt;5% of patients in Trial 201 included anaphylactic reaction (12%) and pain (8%); in Trial 12-230 SAEs in &gt;5% of patients included hypertension (14%), hypotension (11%), and pyrexia (8%).
L1765: Naxitamab can cause serious infusion-related reactions and neurologic toxicity. Given the potential severity of these adverse reactions and the occurrence of infusion related reactions and pain in most patients in either trial, a boxed warning was included in the product label.
L1766: Serious infusion reactions required urgent intervention including fluid resuscitation, administration of bronchodilators and corticosteroids, intensive care unit admission, infusion rate reduction or interruption of naxitamab infusion. Components of these infusion reactions included hypotension, bronchospasm, hypoxia, wheezing, and stridor. Infusion reactions of any Grade occurred in 100% of patients in Trial 201 and 94% of patients in Trial 12-230. In Study 201, 68% of patients in Trial 201 experienced a Grade 3 infusion reaction; while in Study 12-230, 29% of patients experienced a Grade 3 infusion reaction and 1.4% experienced a Grade 4 infusion reaction. One patient in Study 12-230 (1.4%) experienced a cardiac arrest 1.5 hours following completion of naxitamab infusion. Anaphylaxis occurred in 12% of patients in Study 201. The product label contains detailed instructions for pre-mediations as well as instructions for slowing, interrupting or permanently discontinuing naxitamab infusions based on the severity of the adverse reaction.
L1767: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1768: Neurotoxicity experienced in patients receiving naxitamab included pain, peripheral neuropathy, neurological disorders of the eye, transverse myelitis, and PRES/RPLS. Pain, including abdominal pain, bone pain, neck pain, and extremity pain, occurred in 100% of patients in Study 201 and 94% of patients in Study 12-230. Grade 3 pain occurred in 72% of patients in Study 201.
L1769: Peripheral neuropathy including peripheral sensory neuropathy, peripheral motor neuropathy, paresthesia, and neuralgia occurred in 32% of patients in Trial 201 and in 24% of patients in Trial 12-230. Neurological disorders of the eye occurred in 19% of patients in Trial 12-230 and 24% of patients in Trial 201; 8% of patients in Study 201 experienced an event that had not resolved based on the data cut-off of the 120-day safety update. Urinary retention occurred in 4% of patients in Trial 201 and Trial 12-230 and in some patients persisted beyond discontinuation of opioids. Transverse myelitis occurred in a patient receiving naxitamab outside of a clinical study. RPLS occurred in 2 (2.8%) patients in Trial 12-230. The product label describes neurotoxicity as a warning, and provides guidance on dose modifications or discontinuations
L1770: Hypertension occurred in 44% of patients in Study 201 and 28% of patients in Study 12-230 who received naxitamab. Grade 3 hypertension occurred in 4% of patients in Trial 201 and 7% of patients in Trial 12-230 and led to permanent discontinuation of naxitamab in some patients. In both studies, most events occurred on the day of a naxitamab infusion and occurred up to 9 days from an infusion. The product label describes this risk and the need for extended monitoring of blood pressure.
L1771: The most common adverse reactions in Trial 201 (\ge 25\%) were pain, infusion-related reaction, tachycardia, cough, vomiting, diarrhea, nausea, hypertension, peripheral neuropathy, urticaria, pyrexia, headache, edema, sinus tachycardia, and fatigue. The most common grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities (\ge 5\%) were decreased neutrophils, decreased lymphocytes, decreased platelet count, increased alanine aminotransferase and decreased potassium.
L1772: The most common adverse reactions (\ge 25\%) in Trial 12-230 were infusion-related reaction, pain, vomiting, cough, nausea, diarrhea, decreased appetite, sinus tachycardia, fatigue, erythema multiforme, hypertension, injection site reaction, anxiety, irritability, and localized edema. The most common grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities (\ge 5\%) were decreased lymphocytes, decreased neutrophils, decreased hemoglobin, decreased platelet count, decreased potassium, increased alanine aminotransferase, decreased glucose, decreased sodium, decreased albumin, and decreased phosphate.
L1773: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1774: Clinically relevant adverse reactions in \le 10\% of patients who received naxitamab with GM-CSF in either study included apnea, hypopnea, generalized edema, peripheral edema, and device related infection. Events of apnea, hypopnea, unresponsiveness and somnolence also occurred in some patients and may be related to concomitant administration of opioids or to co-occurring events of severe infusion reactions, such as severe hypotension.
L1775: The safety population included patients ages 1 to 24 years of age across both trials, with a median age of 5 years in both trials. Although relatively few young adult patients and very young patients (2 years of age or younger) were enrolled, exploratory analyses of safety trends did not demonstrate a significant difference in safety across groups.
L1776: Overall, the risks associated with naxitamab appear acceptable in the setting of patients with relapsed or refractory neuroblastoma who have no approved therapies. Naxitamab is expected to be administered in infusion centers with intensive monitoring and the ability to quickly respond to events such as severe infusion reactions. In addition, the existence of previously approved products in the US (dinutuximab) with a similar mechanism of action and toxicity profile means that the majority of pediatric oncologists administering naxitamab will have familiarity with expected toxicities associated with anti-GD2 antibodies. FDA assessed that the communication of risk in the product label required the addition of a boxed warning given the potential severity of the reactions.
L1777: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L1778: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L1779: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1780: There were two major statistical issues in the review of this application.
L1781: The first statistical review issue was that in Trial 12-230, each patient may have had multiple enrollments in the study. To avoid collinearity in the assessment of response in the same patients over multiple enrollments, only the data from the eligible first enrollment was considered. This limitation to the sample size in addition to the clinical consideration to consider only patients with baseline evaluable disease who initiated at least one infusion of 3.0 mg/kg or greater of naxitamab (as opposed to having completed at least one cycle as proposed by the Applicant) resulted in a final sample size of 38 patients in Trial 12-230.
L1782: The second statistical review issue was regarding the follow-up time in Trial 12-230. Given the structure of the assessments for the study reduced the possibility of longer follow-up for DOR, the inference that can be made from the study data is limited. In general, FDA is unable to determine whether the observed results for DOR reflect the actual duration of the treatment effect or whether the results underestimate the duration of response due to censoring. Therefore, both the observed proportion of responders with response ongoing at 6 months (26 weeks) was presented in addition to the Kaplan-Meier estimates.
L1783: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L1784: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1785: Patients with relapsed and refractory neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a response of partial response, minor response, or stable disease to prior therapy represent a population with a serious and life-threatening rare disease for which there is no FDA-approved therapy and no known curative therapy.
L1786: In the opinion of the review team, the submitted evidence meets the statutory evidentiary standard for accelerated approval. The recommendation for accelerated approval is based on the results from two single-arm, open-label trials.
L1787: The trials enrolled patients with relapsed or refractory high risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow. The primary efficacy population included patients with relapsed or refractory disease in the bone or bone marrow who had demonstrated a response of partial response, minor response or stable disease, to prior therapy, had evaluable disease per central review according to the revised International Neuroblastoma Response Criteria (INRC), and who
L1788: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1789: received at least one dose of naxitamab. The primary efficacy population included 22 patients in Trial 201 and 38 patients in Trial 12-230.
L1790: The analysis of efficacy demonstrated a clinically meaningful ORR per independent review with sufficient duration to indicate a clinical benefit in this relapsed and refractory population. It should be noted that patients in both trials were permitted to receive radiation in certain circumstances, though assessment of response excluded sites that received radiation. Patients for whom radiation was administered to the only site of evaluable disease, which applied only to patients in Trial 12-230, were excluded from the efficacy analysis. Patients with a best response of progressive disease to initial therapy or therapy at relapse were excluded from Trial 201 and from the efficacy analysis population in Trial 12-230. Therefore, the indication the review team proposes for approval also excludes these patients.
L1791: The confirmed ORR in patients with metastatic, relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a response of stable disease, partial response, or minor response to prior therapy in Trial 201 (n=22) per blinded, independent review was 45% (95% CI: 24%, 68%), with 30% of the 10 responders having observed DOR of 6 months. The primary efficacy analysis in Trial 201 included 4 patients with prior anti-GD2 treatment. One (25%) of these patients demonstrated a response.
L1792: The confirmed ORR in patients with metastatic, relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a response of stable disease, partial response, or minor response to prior therapy in Trial 12-230 (n=38) per blinded, independent review according to the revised INRC was 34% (95% CI: 20%, 51%), with 23% of the 13 responders having observed DOR of 6 months. The primary efficacy analysis in Trial 12-230 included 22 patients with prior anti-GD2 treatment. Four (18%) of these patients demonstrated a response (95% CI 5%, 40%). Given the limited number of patients, the point estimate for these results needs to be interpreted with caution; however, the results suggest that naxitamab has some clinical activity in patients with prior anti-GD2 antibody therapy. The difference in ORR between the two trials may be explained by the higher percentage of relapsed patients, who are more heavily pre-treated, in Trial 12-230 (55% vs. 36% in Trial 201).
L1793: During the review, the review team considered the impact of a short “wash-out” period between prior systemic therapy and the timing of baseline disease assessments for both studies. FDA considered whether the short time from completion of systemic therapy until the baseline disease assessment could confound the assessment of response to naxitamab. In practice, the urgency to enroll patients, particularly with primary refractory disease who may have demonstrated stable disease, minor response or partial response to prior intensive chemotherapy, onto a clinical trial may render a standard washout period from prior therapy
L1794: (i.e., at least 4 weeks prior to baseline disease assessment) challenging. Since patients with progressive disease were excluded from the trial, patients with relapsed disease were treated first with other therapy (typically chemotherapy or immunotherapy) prior to enrollment, thus the concern that the effect of prior therapy is not reflected in the baseline disease assessment (and thus that responses observed in the trial cannot wholly be attributed to naxitamab) is greater in this population. FDA performed a sensitivity analysis removing patients with a short interval (&lt; 2.5 weeks) between systemic therapy and baseline disease assessment, which resulted in similar ORRs in comparison to those noted above in both trials, and also resulted in the exclusion of durable complete responses that the review team did not consider reasonably attributable to prior therapy. FDA's approach was therefore to exclude patients with no washout period between systemic therapy and baseline disease assessments, which applied to one patient in Trial 12-230 as described in the review.
L1795: Assessment of duration of response was limited by administration of additional anticancer therapies subsequent to achieving a complete or partial response in patients in both trials. Neither study was designed to include scheduled assessments of response after completion of treatment with naxitamab, and Trial 201 is currently ongoing. Additional clinical data from Trial 201 may provide additional data to inform durability of responses.
L1796: Although the two trials supporting efficacy and safety in this application used different manufacturing processes, OBP determined that the products used in each trial were generally comparable, although direct comparability of potency was not possible given differences in potency assays used for each product. Refer to OBP assessment memo, p. 32, for details.
L1797: Given the rarity of relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma and the lack of an approved or standard treatment, a randomized trial may not be feasible. The review team considers that the ORR in the two trials, along with the observed duration of responses, in patients treated with naxitamab is sufficient to establish clinical benefit in the rare group of patients with high risk neuroblastoma. Data from additional patients in this patient population including overall response rate and duration of response, may be required to confirm clinical benefit.
L1798: The safety of naxitamab in combination with GM-CSF was evaluated in a subpopulation of 97 patients with high-risk neuroblastoma in bone or bone marrow who had relapsed or refractory disease, or were in complete remission, from the two open-label, single arm studies used to support efficacy, Trials 201 and 12-230; patients in the primary safety population received the recommended phase 2 dose (3 mg/kg/day) of naxitamab intravenously on days 1, 3, and 5 of each cycle and GM-CSF 250 \mu\text{g}/\text{m}^2/\text{day} subcutaneously on Days -4 to 0 and GM-CSF 500 \mu\text{g}/\text{m}^2/\text{day} subcutaneously on Days 1 to 5. Infusion-related reactions and neurotoxicity were prominent and potentially severe safety signals evaluated during the review.
L1799: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1800: Nearly all patients in both studies (100% of patients in Study 201 and 94% of patients in Study 12-230) experienced an infusion reaction, as defined by a pooled term analysis of events as described in Section 8.2.5. In Study 201, 68% of patients experienced a Grade 3 infusion reaction; while in Study 12-230, 32% of patients experienced a Grade 3 infusion reaction and 1.4% experienced a Grade 4 infusion reaction. One patient in Study 12-230 (1.4%) experienced a cardiac arrest 1.5 hours following completion of an infusion of naxitamab. Given the prevalence and severity of infusion-related reactions, as well as the need for careful monitoring and management, IRRs are included as a black box warning in the product label.
L1801: Neurotoxicity experienced by patients who received naxitamab included pain, peripheral neuropathy, prolonged urinary retention, transverse myelitis, neurological disorders of the eye, and reversible posterior leukoencephalopathy syndrome. Pain occurred in 100% of patients in Study 201 and 94% of patients in Study 12-230. Grade 3 pain occurred in 72% of patients in Study 201. The USPI advises premedication to treat neuropathic pain. Peripheral neuropathy including sensory neuropathy, motor neuropathy, paresthesia and neuralgia occurred in 32% of patients in Study 201 and in 24% of patients in Study 12-230. Neurological disorders of the eye including mydriasis and blurred vision occurred in 19% of patients in Study 12-230 and 24% of patients in Study 201, and in some cases were prolonged and had not resolved as of the data cut-off for the BLA. Prolonged urinary retention beyond discontinuation of opioids, transverse myelitis, and RPLS were also reported in patients who received naxitamab. Given the severity of these reactions in some patients, the prevalence of events of neurotoxicity, and the need for careful monitoring, neurotoxicity was included as a black box warning in the product label.
L1802: Hypotension was a prominent component of infusion-related reactions, but hypertension was also observed in patients who received naxitamab, in some cases associated with RPLS or causing permanent discontinuation of naxitamab. Hypertension occurred in 44% of patients in Study 201 and 28% of patients in Study 12-230 who received naxitamab. Grade 3 hypertension occurred in 4% of patients in Study 201 and 7% of patients in Study 12-230. Hypertension is included as a warning in the product label.
L1803: The most common adverse reactions in Studies 201 and 12-230 (&gt; 25% in either study) were infusion-related reaction, pain, tachycardia, vomiting, cough, nausea, diarrhea, decreased appetite, hypertension, fatigue, erythema multiforme, peripheral neuropathy, urticaria, pyrexia, headache, injection site reaction, edema, anxiety, localized edema and irritability. The most common Grade 3 or 4 laboratory abnormalities (\ge 5\% in either study) were decreased lymphocytes, decreased neutrophils, decreased hemoglobin, decreased platelet count, decreased potassium, increased alanine aminotransferase, decreased glucose, decreased calcium, decreased albumin, decreased sodium and decreased phosphate.
L1804: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1805: The safety profile of naxitamab demonstrated the potential for severe infusion-related reactions and neurotoxicity, and the review team felt that a black box warning was indicated to advise providers of these risks. However, the review team considers that naxitamab may be safely delivered to patients with appropriate guidance in the product label, oversight from trained pediatric oncology providers who are experienced in managing similar toxicities, and administration in infusion centers equipped to respond rapidly to infusion-related reactions. The clinical reviewers do not recommend a REMS be implemented for naxitamab given these considerations. Risk management based on labeling, including a black box warning, and routine pharmacovigilance will be employed to ensure the safe and effective use of naxitamab.
L1806: The safety population included patients 1 to 24 years of age across both studies, with a median age of 5 years in both studies. Although relatively few young adult patients and very young patients (2 years of age or younger) were enrolled, exploratory analyses of safety trends did not demonstrate a significant difference in safety across groups.
L1807: In summary, the benefit: risk assessment is favorable for the use of naxitamab for the treatment of patients with relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a response of partial response, minor response or stable disease to prior therapy. Naxitamab has demonstrated significant and clinically meaningful evidence of anti-tumor activity including confirmed ORR and evidence of durability of responses in previously treated patients with high risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow. The safety profile of naxitamab is acceptable given the serious and life-threatening nature of relapsed and refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow, evidence of substantial anti-tumor activity, and the lack of approved therapies in this population.
L1808: The reviewers recommend accelerated approval under Subpart E (21 CFR 601.41) for naxitamab, in combination with GM-CSF, for the treatment of patients with relapsed or refractory high-risk neuroblastoma in the bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a response of stable disease, partial response or minor response to prior therapy. Accelerated approval is recommended given the small sample size and need for additional multicenter data to confirm the efficacy and safety of naxitamab. Clinical benefit will be verified and further characterized by submission of data on response rate and durability of response from additional patients who will enroll across multiple sites in the ongoing clinical trial, Study 201.

L1817: ## 9 Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L1818: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1819: The Division did not refer this BLA to the Oncologic Drug Advisory Committee (ODAC) because, in the setting of the boxed warning and other advice in the product labeling, the safety profile is acceptable for the treatment of the indicated population and the application did not raise significant public health questions regarding the role of naxitamab for this indication. Outside expertise was not necessary as there were no controversial issues that could benefit from an ODAC discussion.

L1821: ## 10 Pediatrics
L1822: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1823: Neuroblastoma nearly exclusively affects young children: 60% of cases occur before 2 years of age, and 97% occur by 10 years of age.[2] Naxitamab for the treatment of neuroblastoma was granted a Rare Pediatric Disease Designation by FDA. In addition, naxitamab for the treatment of neuroblastoma has orphan drug status; therefore, naxitamab is exempt from the requirements of PREA.
L1824: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1825: FDA agrees that naxitamab is exempt from the requirements of PREA. Please see described demographics for the safety and efficacy populations in Sections 8.1 and 8.2.

L1830: Summary of Significant Labeling Changes (High level changes and not direct quotations)

Consistent with the FDA Guidance: Warnings and Precautions, Contraindications, and Boxed Warning Sections of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products guidance, these serious adverse reactions were described in a boxed warning because they can be prevented, reduced in frequency, or severity by appropriate use of the product.

L1850: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L1851: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1852: The following postmarketing requirements and commitments were requested by FDA and agreed upon by the Applicant. Please also refer to the approval letter for the agreed-upon timeline for submission of PMR/PMCs and for CMC PMRs.
L1853: ## Nonclinical PMR/PMC
L1854: PMR# 3950-3
L1855: ## Rationale for PMR:
L1856: There are outstanding questions related to the toxicity of naxitamab, particularly regarding neuropathic pain and nerve damage associated with the use of the antibody and reversibility of such outcomes in the indicated patient population. Due to the lack of changes in nociception in the nonclinical toxicology studies provided in the BLA and the age of the animals used, an additional toxicology study in juvenile animals of a relevant species is needed to adequately characterize the risk of the use of naxitamab on the central and peripheral nerves as related to the indicated pediatric population.
L1857: ## PMR Description:
L1858: Conduct a repeat-dose juvenile animal toxicology study (a minimum of 2 cycles) in a pharmacologically relevant species that is also relevant for the toxicological analysis of an antibody with immune-mediated effects that will measure the toxicity associated with the use of naxitamab, particularly effects on the central and peripheral nervous system. Administration of naxitamab should be reflective of the clinical administration schedule. Incorporate an evaluation of the effect of treatment on the proximal and distal nerves, and evaluation of the C1 level of the spinal cord in this study and include 7-8 slices for histopathological assessment of the brain. Evaluate the potential for effects on nociception and pain threshold, both acute and long-term, both during the study and at the end of an appropriate recovery period.
L1859: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1860: ## Clinical Pharmacology PMR/PMC
L1861: PMC# 3950-4
L1862: ## Rationale for PMC:
L1863: The BLA for naxitamab included five clinical studies (12-230, 11-009, 12-116, 2PRO1, and 201), with trials 12-230, 11-009, and 12-116 conducted by MSKCC, with study 12-230 considered to be the pivotal trial for both the safety and efficacy of Naxitamab. Study 2PRO1 and Trial 201 are currently being conducted by the Applicant and are evaluating the efficacy and safety of naxitamab in the indicated patient population. The immunogenicity of naxitamab has not been adequately characterized as only 25 out of the estimated 85 patients have been enrolled and had data available for review in the currently ongoing Applicant conducted Trial 201. The current data from Study 201 provides reliable pharmacokinetics (PK) and anti-product antibody (ADA) testing results with fully validated PK and ADA assays in a very limited number of patients (n=4). The PK and ADA results from the three trials conducted by MSKCC are deemed not reliable because the PK and ADA assays used in these trials are not fully validated. An immunogenicity testing plan should be incorporated in this trial and any future planned trials to adequately characterize the incidence of ADA and their impact on PK, efficacy and safety of naxitamab and provide reliable information to the product labeling.
L1864: ## PMC Description:
L1865: Conduct immunogenicity testing in all patients enrolled in the ongoing multicenter clinical trial (Trial 201) of naxitamab using the validated assays to adequately characterize the incidence of anti-drug antibody (ADA) and neutralizing antibodies against naxitamab and assess the impact of ADA on the pharmacokinetics (PK), safety and efficacy of naxitamab in accordance with FDA Guidance for Industry: Immunogenicity Assessment for Therapeutic Protein Products available at https://www.fda.gov/downloads/drugs/guidances/ucm338856.pdf. Submit the integrated immunogenicity assessment report in the final report submission. The results from this study may inform product labeling.
L1866: ## Clinical PMRs
L1867: PMR #3950-1
L1868: ## Rationale for PMR:
L1869: Naxitamab is being considered under accelerated approval for the treatment of patients with relapsed or refractory neuroblastoma in bone or bone marrow who have
L1870: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L1871: demonstrated a response of stable disease or better following initial or subsequent therapy. Approval is based upon the results of a single center study and an interim analysis for an ongoing multicenter study. Continued approval may be contingent upon verification and description of clinical benefit through submission of additional efficacy data from additional patients in the ongoing multicenter clinical trial (Study 201).
L1872: ## PMR Description:
L1873: Submit the final report, including datasets, from an ongoing multicenter clinical trial to verify and further characterize the clinical benefit of naxitamab for the treatment of patients with relapsed or refractory neuroblastoma in combination with granulocyte-macrophage colony-stimulating factor (GM-CSF) in bone or bone marrow who have demonstrated a partial response, minor response, or stable disease to prior therapy; and to provide a more precise estimation of overall response rate and duration of response (according to the revised International Neuroblastoma Response Criteria by blinded independent review). Enroll a minimum of 80 patients with evaluable disease and systematically follow all responding patients for at least 12 months from the onset of response, or until disease progression, whichever comes first. Include subgroup analyses of overall response rate and duration of response by prior anti-GD2 exposure, disease status (refractory vs. relapsed disease), anti-drug antibody (ADA) status (neutralizing antibody [nAb] positive versus nAb negative, ADA positive versus ADA negative), and area of disease involvement (bone marrow vs. bone) in the final report reflecting a sufficient number of patients in relevant subgroups. Derive the clinical data from a minimum of six study sites, with at least five patients enrolled per site in at least five sites.
L1874: PMR# 3950-2
L1875: ## Rationale for PMR:
L1876: Serious infusion reactions have occurred with naxitamab, necessitating a boxed warning. The product labeling included the following terms, occurring the day of or the day following infusion, to capture serious infusion reactions: hypotension, bronchospasm, flushing, wheezing, stridor, urticaria, dyspnea, pyrexia, infusion related reaction, face edema, swelling face, (b) (4), periorbital edema, lip edema, lip swelling, tongue edema, swollen tongue, mouth swelling, (b) (4), respiratory tract edema, chills, hypoxia, pruritis, erythema multiforme, and (b) (4). The majority of the safety data is derived from a single center, where the product was developed and management of these serious reactions at this center may not be representative of experience at additional centers. Post-marketing data will not be
L1877: sufficient to characterize the risk given the level of detail available regarding management of infusion reactions such as premedication, rescue medications, infusion interruptions and discontinuation of naxitamab. Due to the limited, single-arm nature of the safety database of naxitamab at the time of this accelerated approval, additional clinical data from the ongoing multicenter study is needed to adequately characterize the risk in a broader, multicenter experience.
L1878: ## PMR Description:
L1879: Conduct a comprehensive analysis evaluating and characterizing the incidence, clinical presentation, management, and outcome of the potential serious risk of naxitamab-associated infusion-related reactions in a sufficient number of patients to adequately characterize the risk. Submit an integrated final report containing data using the preferred terms included in product labelling from patient-level and pooled analyses of on-going and completed clinical trials, post-marketing reports and/or literature reports and a comprehensive pharmacovigilance assessment for this potential serious risk. The report should also include an analysis of pre-medication administered. The results from this report may inform product labelling.
L1880: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

L1954: ## 19.4. **OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)**
L1955: ## 19.4.1 Population PK Analysis
L1956: The Applicant conducted a population PK analysis to characterize the PK of naxitamab, identify covariate factors that could affect naxitamab disposition and compare the individual exposure estimates for subsequent exposure-response analyses. Data were collected across the trials 11-009, 12-116 and 12-230 conducted at the Memorial Sloan Kettering Cancer Center (MSKCC) and Trial 201 conducted by the Applicant. The studies included in the population PK analysis and the PK sampling times are shown in Table 19-3.
L1957: Table 19-3. Overview of studies included in the population PK analysis.
L1958: Trial ID	Trial Title	Dose Level (per dose)	N Subjects with PK Data	PK Sampling
11-009	Phase I trial of Humanized 3F8 Monoclonal Antibody (hu3F8) in subjects with High-Risk Neuroblastoma and GD2-Positive Tumors	0.1 to 1.5 mg/kg on day 1 and 8	23	predose and at 5 minutes and 3, 6 to 8, 24, 48, 72, 96, 120, and 168 h post-infusion
12-016	Phase I trial of Humanized 3F8 Monoclonal Antibody (hu3F8) when combined with IL-2 in subjects with High-Risk Neuroblastoma and GD2-Positive Solid Tumors	0.3 to 1.2 mg/kg on day 1 and 8	12	predose and at 5 minutes and 3, 6 to 8, 24, 48, 72, 96, 120, and 168 h post-infusion
12-230	Phase I/II trial of Combination Therapy of Antibody hu3F8 with GM-CSF in subjects with Relapsed/Refractory High-Risk Neuroblastoma	0.3 to 3.2 mg/kg per dose on day 1, 3 and 5	55	predose and at 5 minutes and 3, 6 to 8, 24, 48, 72, 96, 120, 168, 216 and 264 h post-infusion
201	Pivotal Phase II Trial of Naxitamab (hu3F8) and GMCSF in High-Risk Neuroblastoma Patients with Primary Refractory Disease or Incomplete Response to Salvage Treatment in Bone and/or Bone Marrow	3 mg/kg per infusion on D1, D3 and D5 of each cycle	23	Cycle 1: predose, 15 min (during infusion D1&D3), EOI and at 10 min, 1, 3, 6, 24, 168, 264, 384 and 552 h post-infusion.
From cycle 2 onwards: predose (D1)
L1959: N: Number of. In several subjects, PK samples were also obtained at 1 and 2 hours post-infusion on day 1 and 8 in trial 11-009

L1961: The majority of patients in the primary patient population were white (74.3%) and 14.2% were Asian. The majority (n=82, 72.6%) of the 113 patients were of normal liver function, 30 patients (26.5%) with mild hepatic impairment, and 1 (0.9%) with moderate hepatic impairment based on NCI hepatic impairment classification. The majority of the 113 patients had normal (n=99, 87.6%) or mildly impaired (n=13, 11.5%) renal function, and 1 (0.9%) had moderate renal impairment. No patients with severe renal
