L1968: *Source: The Applicant's population PK report (hu3F8-MS-02), Page 27, Table 2.*
L1969: The population PK analysis was conducted via nonlinear mixed-effects modeling with the NONMEM software, version 7.4 using first-order conditional estimation with INTERACTION option (FOCE+I). Concentrations below the limit of quantification (BLQ) were excluded from the PK analysis. The PK samples obtained in MSKCC trials 11-009, 12-116 and 12-230 were analyzed with a partially-validated assay and the LLOQ was not defined. For Trial 201, a validated assay with a LLOQ of 70 ng/mL was used. This LLOQ of 70 ng/mL was also considered for the MSKCC trials. In total, 50 observations were reported as concentration below BLQ, which were excluded from the analysis.
L1970: A total of 1743 PK observations from 113 patients were included in the population PK analysis. The plasma concentrations of naxitamab were described by a four-compartment disposition model with linear elimination. The model included inter-individual variability (IIV) for CL and V_1. The residual error model included proportional error terms. Covariates included sex, race, age, BW, BMI, BSA, LBM, ALT status, AP status, eGFR status, IL-2 coadministration and study effect.
L1971: The final population PK parameter estimates and bootstrap estimates for naxitamab are presented in Table 19-5. The final PK model was parameterized in terms of CL, V_1, Q_1, V_2, Q_2, V_3, Q_3, and V_4. Estimated fixed and random effect parameters were estimated with good precision (%RSE &lt; 16%). The magnitude of the interindividual variability was moderate for CL (30.2 %CV) but high for V_1 (100.8 %CV). Residual variability was moderate. The PK of naxitamab is affected by BW, IL-2 coadministration and study effect. Study effect on CL is likely a combination of different infusion times, lack of actual dosing and sampling time points in the MSKCC trials, lack of PK samples obtained during the terminal phase in the MSKCC trials, and the use of different bioanalytical assays in the MSKCC trials and Trial 201. BW was included in the final population PK model following allometric principles, where volume and clearance parameters increase less proportional to body weight (exponents are 0.577 and 0.323, respectively).
L1972: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}

The model described the observed data well and the model predictions were generally within the 90% prediction intervals. No apparent bias was observed in the overall model fit for the data. Bootstrap results are in good concordance with the final model parameter estimates. (Table 19-4)

Naxitamab exposures of AUC_{0-672h} and C_{max} are expected to increase with increasing BW as a result of the proportional increase in total administered dose and less than proportional increase in naxitamab CL (Figure 19-3, Table 19-6, Figure 19-4 and Table 19-7).

L2004: ADA formation was tested for 157 patients in Trials 11-009, 12-116, 12-230 (Phase 1 only) and 201 (Table 19-8). The ADA samples obtained in Trial 201 were analyzed using a multi-tiered approach consisting of screening, confirmatory and neutralizing antibody testing with validated assays, and samples were categorized as either negative or positive. The samples obtained in MSKCC trials (11-009, 12-116 and 12-230) were analyzed with a partially-validated screening assay only.

L2010: In total, ADA formation was observed in 40 patients (25.5%) at any time during treatment with naxitamab; however, only a small subgroup (n=18) of patients, providing both (positive) ADA data and naxitamab PK data at the day of ADA formation or thereafter during Cycle 1, was available for the evaluation of the impact of ADA formation on the naxitamab PK. The ADA effect was not evaluated in the population PK analysis.
L2011: In Trial 201, positive ADA titers were observed in 2 patients (8.7% out of 23 patients), with a total of 4 PK samples obtained at the same day or after positive ADA titers were observed. Figure 19-5 showed the individual naxitamab PK profiles. In general, reduced naxitamab concentrations during the first treatment cycle were observed in patients with ADA formation for which PK data were available at the time of ADA formation. Because PK data are not available for subsequent treatment cycles in these patients, it is uncertain whether the naxitamab exposure changes were due to ADA formation.

L2015: ## **Reviewer's comments:**
L2016: *The population PK model developed by the applicant was verified by the reviewer. The model appears to be acceptable in general because of the good agreement between observations and predictions. Based on the population PK model, naxitamab exposures are expected to increase with increasing BW (Figure 19-4). The mean predicted AUC_{0-672h} is approximately doubled in patients with BW 70-80 kg compared to the patients with BW 20-30 kg. (Table 19-6). Flat dosing in adults and older adolescents with higher BW will minimize PK variability. There were limited clinical safety experiences in patients with BW &gt; 50 kg with doses &gt; 450 mg per cycle (i.e., 3 patients with BW &gt;50 kg received dose &gt;450 mg/cycle with 2 patients each only received 3 infusions and 1 patient received 5 infusions) who are expected to have higher exposure compared to that observed in the clinical trials. A dosing cap of 450 mg per cycle of naxitamab for patients with BW &gt; 50 kg was suggested by the reviewer. With the dosing cap, predicted naxitamab exposures for patients with BW higher than 50 kg are comparable to patients with lower BW (30 - 50 kg) at weight-based dosing of 3 mg/kg/day (9 mg/kg per cycle). (Figure 19-6)*
L2017: Figure 19-6. Simulated naxitamab exposures versus bodyweight with dosing cap of 450 mg per cycle.
L2018: Source: Reviewer's analysis
L2019: ## 19.4.2 Exposure-Response for Efficacy Analysis
L2020: The exposure-response relationships of overall response rate (ORR) or complete response (CR) and naxitamab exposure were evaluated with 22 patients in trials 201 and 17 patients in Trial 12-230. Neither naxitamab exposure (AUC), nor any of the evaluated covariates were found to have a significant effect on ORR or CR. Average daily naxitamab exposure in Cycle 1 was comparable between responders and non-responders. Patients without prior anti-GD2 treatment had a slightly higher ORR or CR rate compared to those with prior anti-GD2 treatment, and ORR/CR rates were higher in Trial 201 compared to Trial 12-230. (Table 19-9)

L2026: The results of the exposure-efficacy analysis for naxitamab were verified by the reviewer. Overall, the ER relationships between ORR/CR and exposures of naxitamab are inconclusive because of the limited data from the trials in small number of patients and potential confounding factors (e.g., ADA effect).

L2046: ## **Reviewer's comments:**
L2047: The results of the exposure-safety analysis for naxitamab were verified by the reviewer. Additional analyses were conducted for AEs (Grade 2+ bronchospasm, hypertension, hypotension and Grade 3 hypotension) by the reviewer with additional covariates of prior anti-GD2 treatment and study effect (trials 11-009, 12-116 and 12-230 conducted by MSK vs study 201 conducted by the Applicant). Study effect was included in the analysis because higher AE (Grade 2+ bronchospasm, hypertension and hypotension) rates were observed in Trial 201 compared with other trials (Table 19-9). As shown in Table 19-9, patients in MSK trials (Trial 11-009, 12-116, 12-230) had lower occurrence rates of Grade 2+ bronchospasm than patients in the Y-mAbs-sponsored trial (Trial 201). Figure 19-10 and Figure 19-11 indicate that the patients with prior anti-GD2 treatment might have lower Grade 2+ bronchospasm rate than patients without prior anti-GD2 treatment and a possible relationship between naxitamab AUC_{24h} and occurrence of bronchospasm events, but both were not identified in the analysis.
L2048: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L2049: Figure 19-10. Grade 2+ bronchospasm stratified by prior Anti-GD2 treatment.
L2050: Data points depict the incidence rates of bronchospasm stratified by prior Anti-GD2 treatment. Error bars depict the 95% CI of the observed incidence rate.
L2051: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L2052: Figure 19-11. Logistic regression of grade 2+ bronchospasm with naxitamab exposure (AUC_{24h})
L2053: Data points depict the incidence rates of bronchospasm binned by exposure quartiles and plotted versus the predicted mean AUC_{24h} in each bin. Error bars depict the 90% CI of the observed incidence rate.
L2054: Source: Reviewer's analysis.
L2055: From the additional exposure-safety analysis for Grade 2+ hypertension, AUC_{24h} was identified to be the only predictor which is similar to the Applicant's result.
L2056: From the additional exposure-safety analysis for Grade 2+ hypotension, study effect and AUC_{24h} were identified to be the predictors (Figure 19-12). Slightly lower AE rate was observed in the
L2057: {DANYELZA, naxitamab-gqgk}
L2058: patients with prior Anti-GD2 treatment comparing with the patients without prior Anti-GD2 treatment (Figure 19-13). Table 19-9 and Figure 19-14 show that patients in MSK trials (Trial 11-009, 12-116, 12-230) had lower occurrence rates of Grade 3 hypotension than patients in the Y-mabs trial (Trial 201). Figure 19-15 illustrates that there is a possible relationship between naxitamab AUC_{24h} and occurrence of grade 3 hypotension, but it was not identified in the analysis.
